# mdmate

## Problem

Good inline Markdown editing is tied to heavy note-taking apps or locked inside other editors. Fast editors like SublimeText show raw Markdown, and viewers can't edit. There is no small desktop app that opens a Markdown file instantly, renders it where you type, and keeps the file on disk exactly as you wrote it.

## Vision

You double-click a README and it is on screen before you have finished looking at it, rendered and ready to type in. The syntax appears only where your caret is. Tabs, a folder sidebar and free splits feel like SublimeText, and a 1 MB file feels the same as a short note. Rename a file and every link to it follows. Close the app mid-sentence, open it tomorrow, and your unsaved words are still there, and git shows only the lines you meant to change.

## Out of Scope

- **Math and Mermaid.** Later, as separately loaded chunks.
- **Wiki links.** Standard Markdown links only.
- **Multi-cursor.** Ctrl+click is reserved for links.
- **A reading mode.** Live preview and raw source cover it.
- **Spellcheck.** Left out of v1.
- **Table cell editing.** Tables are read-only until the caret reveals their source.
- **Multi-window.** One instance, one window.
- **Auto-update, accounts, telemetry.** None in v1.
- **A theme designer beyond a color list.** Themes are files; Phase 8 adds a plain list of the colors with a text field and a picker for each (ISC-381 to ISC-383), and nothing that previews, imports or shares a theme.
- **Export to HTML or PDF, plugins, sync, AI features.** Different products.
- **macOS and Linux installers in v1.** The code stays portable; only Windows ships.

## Principles

- The file on disk belongs to the writer. The app displays Markdown and never rewrites it.
- Speed is a feature with numbers, measured on real files.
- A document someone else wrote is untrusted input.
- Vue owns the chrome and CodeMirror owns the text.
- Tools enforce what they can; prose holds what they can't.

## Constraints

- Tauri 2, Rust, Vue 3, TypeScript, Tailwind 4, shadcn-vue on Reka UI, Vite, pnpm. `derived_from: principal_stated_goal compound`
- CodeMirror 6 written from scratch. The Markdown text is the document; no parse-and-serialize model.
- All colors, fonts and spacing come from design tokens.
- All OS-specific code lives behind `src-tauri/src/platform/`.
- All file access goes through our own Rust commands.
- Windows 11 ships first, on WebView2.
- **Speed gate.** Four speed claims are gates: ISC-4 (startup 1500 ms), ISC-5 (open 1 MB, 100 ms), ISC-6 (tab switch, 50 ms) and ISC-7 (typing p95 16 ms on the 1 MB fixture). When a gate is measured and missed in the release build, the work stops at that task. Profile once to find the cost, then write the numbers and the cause in the phase's `decisions.md` and hand them to the owner, who decides between a fix, a lower feature scope (such as a lower plain-source cutoff) or a new target. A gate is never loosened, skipped or re-measured under easier conditions to make it pass. Only the owner closes a missed gate, with the decision recorded in Decisions.

## Goal

"Create a markdown editor that follows the inline editing philosophy from Obsidian and the simplicity of SublimeText". mdmate is a desktop app where Markdown renders inline and reveals its syntax at the caret, with tabs, a sidebar and free splits in the SublimeText style. It meets its measured speed targets, keeps files byte-exact, and is built so macOS and Linux need only new platform implementations.

## Not yet specified


## Test Strategy

Commands: `pnpm test:rust <filter>` runs Cargo tests, `pnpm test:web <file>` runs Vitest, `pnpm perf <scenario>` runs the release-build harness, and `node scripts/check-arch.mjs <rule>` runs structural checks. Web UI rows use Interceptor against `pnpm dev`; real-app rows use `pnpm tauri dev`.

## Features

### F0 · Cross-cutting
Why: these promises hold everywhere, and any single feature could quietly break them.

- [x] ISC-1: Anti: saving an unedited file never changes a byte.
- [x] ISC-2: Anti: an edit never changes bytes outside its range.
- [x] ISC-3: Mixed line-ending files keep each untouched line's ending after an edit.
- [x] ISC-4: The release app is usable within 1500 ms of launch.
- [x] ISC-5: A 1 MB Markdown file opens within 100 ms.
- [x] ISC-6: Switching tabs completes within 50 ms.
- [x] ISC-7: Typing stays under 16 ms per key on the 1 MB fixture.
- [x] ISC-8: Files above 3 MB open as plain source with a notice.
- [x] ISC-9: `cfg(windows)` and `target_os` appear only inside `platform/`.
- [x] ISC-10: `cargo check` passes on macOS in CI.
- [x] ISC-11: `cargo check` passes on Linux in CI.
- [x] ISC-12: `@tauri-apps` imports appear only inside `src/platform/`.
- [x] ISC-13: `src/editor/` imports nothing from Vue.
- [x] ISC-14: No hard-coded colors exist outside token files.
- [ ] ISC-15: Anti: `innerHTML` and `v-html` never receive document content.
- [x] ISC-16: The opener refuses every scheme except `http`, `https` and `mailto`.
- [x] ISC-17: Anti: UNC and `file://host/` targets never reach the filesystem.
- [x] ISC-18: Default-app opens ask first unless the file is Markdown or an image.
- [ ] ISC-19: Anti: the hostile fixture causes no code run, launch or SMB attempt.
- [ ] ISC-20: Anti: the app makes no network request except document images.
- [x] ISC-21: Anti: `tauri-plugin-fs` is absent from the dependencies.
- [x] ISC-22: `pnpm check` exits 0.
- [x] ISC-23: `pnpm check:rust` exits 0.
- [x] ISC-24: Anti: no lint suppression lacks a reason comment.
- [x] ISC-173: Anti: an opened workspace can never supply a theme file.
- [x] ISC-555: Anti: no browser shortcut of the web view acts in the window: Ctrl+R, F5, Ctrl+Shift+R, Ctrl+U, F7 and the zoom keys neither reload, show source nor zoom the page.
- [x] ISC-556: The app's own keybindings still work with the browser shortcuts off.

### F1 · Sublime workbench
Why: a dense keyboard-driven layout you can rearrange freely is what separates this from a note app.

- [x] ISC-25: Antecedent: first launch shows titlebar, sidebar, tabs and status bar together.
- [x] ISC-26: Titlebar controls minimize, maximize and close the window.
- [x] ISC-27: Anti: launch never shows a white frame.
- [x] ISC-28: Split right creates side-by-side panes.
- [x] ISC-29: Split down creates stacked panes.
- [x] ISC-30: Splits nest three levels in mixed directions.
- [x] ISC-31: Closing a pane's last tab removes that pane.
- [x] ISC-32: Dragging a tab moves it to another pane.
- [x] ISC-33: Dragging a tab to a pane edge creates a split.
- [x] ISC-34: Dragging a split handle resizes the adjacent panes.
- [x] ISC-35: A keyboard shortcut toggles the sidebar.
- [x] ISC-171: A keyboard shortcut splits the active pane right.
- [x] ISC-172: A keyboard shortcut splits the active pane down.
- [x] ISC-176: Dragging a tab within a strip reorders it.
- [x] ISC-565: A tab has no coloured top edge and has rounded top corners; the strip has padding above the tabs; a tab that is not active has no background of its own, so the strip shows through, and shows the accent while the pointer is on it; and it has a separator on its left edge that CSS alone hides next to the active tab, on the first tab and around a hovered one. (Refined 2026-11-20, see Decisions.)
- [x] ISC-566: Dark, Light and Sepia define the token `tab-separator` and no token for an idle tab; the strip is lighter than the content in Dark and darker than it in Light and Sepia, so the active tab stands out; the accent shows against the strip with readable text on it, and the theme editor lists the tab tokens. (Refined 2026-11-20 and 2026-11-21, see Decisions.)
- [x] ISC-567: In `pnpm dev` the separators, backgrounds, rounding and heights of the strip are as ISC-565 and ISC-566 say, in all three themes.
- [x] ISC-568: The owner finds the tab strip look right in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-582: In Dark the tab strip is lighter than it was, so the active tab, which is the page color, differs from it by more than 1.3 and the text of the idle tabs and the separators still read on it.
- [x] ISC-583: The labels of the sidebar header (Folders, Search, Outline, References) sit on the same baseline as the labels of the tabs, in every built-in theme.
- [x] ISC-584: The selected label of the sidebar header is told from the others by a two pixel underline in the primary color as well as the stronger text color, and the others have neither; selecting another moves both, and no label changes its size or weight.
- [x] ISC-569: A tab can be pinned: the pinned tabs of a pane are always its first tabs, in the order they were pinned; unpinning puts a tab first among the others; a pinned tab stays in the pinned group when moved, keeps its pin in any pane, and the count survives closing and saving.
- [x] ISC-571: A pinned tab shows a pin in the slot of the close button and has no X; unsaved changes show as a smaller dot on the pin's corner that hides while the pointer is on the pin; a click on the pin unpins the tab, also with unsaved changes.
- [x] ISC-572: A dragged pinned tab drops only among the pinned tabs and an unpinned one only after them, and the insertion bar shows where the tab will land.
- [x] ISC-574: In `pnpm dev` the pin, the dot, the hover, the drag and Close All work as ISC-569 to ISC-572 say.
- [x] ISC-575: The owner finds the pin, the dot and the hover behaviour right in the real window.
- [x] ISC-562: A button next to the menu in the title bar toggles the sidebar like the shortcut, reports whether the sidebar is open through its pressed state and tooltip, with one icon in both states, and carries a shadcn tooltip with the action and the current shortcut.
- [x] ISC-601: Ctrl+Tab opens the tab-list menu of the focused pane with the next tab (strip order, pinned first) marked, each further Tab press moves the mark on and wraps after the last tab, and releasing Ctrl switches to the marked tab and closes the menu; Ctrl+Shift+Tab does the same backwards from the previous tab.
- [x] ISC-602: Escape closes the cycle menu with no switch, a pane with one tab does nothing, and the keys act on the focused pane only.
- [x] ISC-603: Ctrl+PageDown and Ctrl+PageUp select the next and previous tab of the focused pane in strip order at once, with no menu, wrapping at the ends and repeating when held.
- [x] ISC-604: In the real window Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+Shift+Tab, Ctrl+PageUp and Ctrl+PageDown reach the page and act as above, and the Keyboard screen lists and rebinds them.
- [x] ISC-663: A split handle is a 1px line at rest; while the pointer is over it or it is dragged it is 4px wide, as wide as the sidebar edge, in the active handle colour, and it goes back to 1px when both end, with no second line left behind.
- [ ] ISC-664: The owner finds the pane handles right in the real window: the 1px line widens on hover, stays wide while dragging, and leaves no second line behind.
- [x] ISC-691: The root viewport locks `overflow: hidden` and `overscroll-behavior: none`, the editor scroller and scrollable chrome panels suppress overscroll chaining, and a wide table's wrapper contains horizontal overscroll without blocking vertical scrolling.
- [x] ISC-692: The owner scrolls past every edge of a document and chrome panels with a precision touchpad in the real window and sees no shifting of the window inside its frame.

### F2 · Reliable documents
Why: nobody trusts an editor twice after it loses their words, so no action here may lose work.

- [x] ISC-36: Opening a file loads its content into a tab.
- [x] ISC-37: Ctrl+S writes edits to disk.
- [x] ISC-38: Tabs with unsaved edits show a dirty marker.
- [x] ISC-39: Files in unsupported encodings open read-only with a notice.
- [x] ISC-40: Anti: a failed save never damages the original file.
- [x] ISC-41: An edit in one pane appears in another pane showing that file.
- [x] ISC-42: Undo from either pane reverts the last edit.
- [x] ISC-43: Closing a pane keeps the document's undo history.
- [x] ISC-44: A disk change reloads a clean tab.
- [x] ISC-45: A disk change on an edited tab raises a conflict choice.
- [x] ISC-46: Anti: mdmate's own save triggers no reload or conflict.
- [x] ISC-47: A force-kill loses nothing up to the last journal write.
- [x] ISC-48: A restart restores tabs and split layout.
- [x] ISC-49: Anti: closing the app with unsaved edits shows no prompt.
- [x] ISC-174: Opening an already open file focuses its tab, and a file is not opened twice within one pane.

### F3 · Inline live preview
Why: this is the reason mdmate exists; you read the rendered page and edit it in place, with syntax only where you are working.

- [x] ISC-50: Headings render without `#` markers away from the caret.
- [x] ISC-51: [container] Emphasis renders with hidden markers.
  - [x] ISC-51.1: Bold renders with hidden markers.
  - [x] ISC-51.2: Italic renders with hidden markers.
  - [x] ISC-51.3: Strikethrough renders with hidden markers.
- [x] ISC-52: Inline code renders with hidden backticks.
- [x] ISC-53: Links render as text with the target hidden.
- [x] ISC-54: List markers render styled.
- [x] ISC-55: Blockquotes render styled.
- [x] ISC-56: Horizontal rules render as lines.
- [x] ISC-57: A caret inside an inline element reveals only that element's syntax.
- [x] ISC-58: Syntax hides again within 16 ms of the caret leaving.
- [x] ISC-59: Anti: the caret never sticks inside hidden syntax.
- [x] ISC-60: Anti: vertical arrow keys never skip a line.
- [x] ISC-61: A per-pane toggle shows raw source.
- [x] ISC-62: Copying a selection copies raw Markdown.
- [x] ISC-63: Decoration time stays flat between 1 MB and 10 MB files.
- [x] ISC-623: A list item written with `-`, `*` or `+` is drawn with an en dash "–" in place of the marker while the selection is away from it; ordered lists, task checkboxes and the text are unchanged.
- [x] ISC-624: The raw marker shows while the selection touches it, the caret never sticks inside the marker, and a copy gives the Markdown text.
- [x] ISC-634: The highlight of a selected whole line spans the text column and no further, so the padding on the left and right of the content area stays unpainted, in plain text, in a code box, in a quote and in a table.
- [x] ISC-635: The owner finds line selections right in the real window: Shift+Down and Ctrl+A over plain text, a code box, a quote and a table, with the line numbers on and off.
- [x] ISC-670: The marker box of a list item is as wide as the en dash drawn in it, so in a proportional font the dash does not overhang the space after it; in a monospace font nothing moves.
- [ ] ISC-671: The owner finds the gap between the dash and the text of a list item right in a proportional font and in a monospace font, in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-678: An inline code span whose whole text is a hex colour of 3, 4, 6 or 8 digits, with its `#`, gets a small colour square before it in that colour; other code spans, fenced code and spans with more text get none.
- [x] ISC-679: The colour square shows whether the selection touches the span or not, the caret never sticks in it, and a copy gives the Markdown text without it.
- [x] ISC-680: A hex colour in inline code inside a table cell gets the same colour square.
- [ ] ISC-681: The owner finds the colour squares right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-694: Inline code text, in body text, in headings and in table cells, is drawn in the foreground colour in Dark, Light and Sepia; the `syntax-code` token and its contrast pair on the code background still exist.
- [x] ISC-695: Headings of levels 1 to 6, and headings in the source view, are drawn in the foreground colour in Dark, Light and Sepia, and differ from body text by size and weight only.
- [ ] ISC-697: The owner finds the code background, the inline code text and the headings right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia.

### F4 · Block preview
Why: documents mix prose with tables, images and code, and a page full of raw syntax blocks breaks the illusion.

- [x] ISC-64: GFM tables render read-only.
- [x] ISC-65: A caret entering a table reveals its raw source.
- [x] ISC-66: Local images render inline from relative paths.
- [x] ISC-67: Remote `https` images render inline.
- [x] ISC-68: YAML frontmatter collapses to a pill.
- [x] ISC-69: Clicking a checkbox changes exactly one byte.
- [x] ISC-70: Fenced code highlights using token colors.
- [x] ISC-71: The entry bundle holds no language parser beyond the ones Markdown itself imports (HTML, CSS and JavaScript); every other language loads when a fence names it.
- [x] ISC-72: Anti: late-rendering widgets never shift the scroll position.
- [x] ISC-180: Anti: an image address other than a relative path (inside the document folder, or inside its workspace folder, ISC-232) or `https` never loads.
- [x] ISC-181: Opening a document lets the page load images from its folder, and from no other folder; a workspace folder adds its own folder (and below) when it is opened, and nothing else does.
- [x] ISC-182: A broken or still-loading image holds a fixed-size placeholder.
- [x] ISC-183: Anti: no selection head ever sits strictly inside a block replacement.
- [x] ISC-184: Anti: a read-only document ignores a checkbox click.
- [x] ISC-185: A fenced code block is drawn as a bordered, rounded container in the light and the dark theme.
- [x] ISC-186: With the caret outside a fenced code block, its fences are hidden and its language shows as a label at the top right.
- [x] ISC-187: With the caret inside a fenced code block, edges included, its fences show and the label is gone.
- [x] ISC-188: Anti: a fenced code block never changes height when the caret enters or leaves it.
- [x] ISC-189: The container is one module that a block of another kind (Mermaid, later) can use.
- [x] ISC-73: Anti: `katex` and `mermaid` are absent from v1 dependencies.
- [x] ISC-554: A table cell with no text keeps its column, so no cell after it shifts left.
- [x] ISC-585: A table in a list item, in a bullet or a numbered list and at any depth, is drawn as a table, starting at the item's text: its indent is kept as an invisible gap as wide as the whitespace it replaces.
- [x] ISC-586: A table in a list item behaves like any table: a press on a cell puts the caret in that cell's source, a caret or a selection on its lines shows them as source, and an edit in the list draws it again or removes it, the same as a scan from scratch would.
- [x] ISC-587: What cannot be drawn without losing its line prefix stays source: a table in a quote or in a list in a quote, and one that starts on the list marker's line; and a top-level table, a picture and a paragraph beside a list are drawn as before.
- [x] ISC-588: The three tables of `ai-docs/phases/phase-00/decisions.md`, which sit under bullets, are drawn in `pnpm dev`, level with the text of their bullets.
- [x] ISC-589: The owner finds the tables in list items right in the real window.
- [x] ISC-674: Table header cells have more vertical padding than a body cell, and the height a table is estimated at counts the taller header.
- [x] ISC-675: A table has rounded outer corners (the radius token) with its borders intact, also in a list item.
- [x] ISC-676: With `editor.stripedTables` on, every second body row of a table is drawn in the stripe colour and the header and the other rows are not; with it off none is, and the change is live.
- [ ] ISC-677: The owner finds the tables right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia: taller header, rounded corners, alternating rows, and the setting turning the rows off.
- [x] ISC-698: The language label of a fenced code block with a container is drawn at its top left, and the top right holds only the copy button.
- [x] ISC-699: Every fenced code block has a copy button, with its fences hidden or shown, with or without a language, and also inside a list item or a quote; an indented code block has none.
- [x] ISC-700: A click on the button puts the text between the fences on the clipboard, with no fence lines and no final line break, for any fence length and line ending, and neither moves the caret nor changes the document.
- [x] ISC-701: After a click the button shows a check mark for about 1.5 s, then the copy icon again; a failed write shows a toast and no check mark.
- [x] ISC-702: The button does not change the arrow-key walk through the block, the height of the block, or what a copy of a selection across the block holds.
- [ ] ISC-703: The owner finds the label and the copy button right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia, in a plain block, a block with a long first line, and blocks in a list and in a quote.
- [x] ISC-710: A theme token for the border of code (`syntax-code-border`) exists in Dark, Light and Sepia and in the theme editor's syntax group with a usage text; it is lighter than the code background in Dark and darker in Light and Sepia, by a contrast of 1.15 to 1.6.
- [x] ISC-711: Inline code, in text and in a table cell, has a 1px border in that token and 2px of padding above and below and 3px at the sides, keeps the size of the text around it, and the part of it a selection covers draws no border.
- [x] ISC-712: A code block draws its border in the same token, and a container of another kind keeps the plain border.
- [ ] ISC-713: The owner finds the border of inline code and of code blocks visible in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia, Dark included.

### F5 · Link navigation
Why: documentation is a web of files, so following a link has to cost one gesture and be undoable.

- [x] ISC-74: Ctrl+click on a relative `.md` link opens it in a new tab.
- [x] ISC-75: Ctrl+click on a `#heading` link jumps to that heading.
- [x] ISC-76: Ctrl+click on `file.md#heading` opens the file at that heading.
- [x] ISC-77: Ctrl+click on a web link opens the default browser.
- [x] ISC-78: A confirmed link to another file opens its default app.
- [x] ISC-79: Links with missing targets show a broken style.
- [x] ISC-80: Alt+Left returns to the previous location.
- [x] ISC-81: Alt+Right moves forward again.
- [x] ISC-82: Anti: a plain click on a link never navigates.

### F6 · Quick editing
Why: formatting should stay in your fingers, so writing never stops for syntax.

- [x] ISC-83: The bold shortcut toggles bold.
- [x] ISC-84: The italic shortcut toggles italic.
- [x] ISC-85: The link shortcut inserts a link.
- [x] ISC-86: The inline code shortcut toggles inline code.
- [x] ISC-87: The strikethrough shortcut toggles strikethrough.
- [x] ISC-88: A shortcut raises the heading level.
- [x] ISC-89: A shortcut lowers the heading level.
- [x] ISC-90: Enter continues a list.
- [x] ISC-91: Enter continues a checkbox list with an unchecked box.
- [x] ISC-92: Enter on an empty item ends the list.
- [x] ISC-93: Tab indents a list item.
- [x] ISC-94: Shift+Tab outdents a list item.
- [x] ISC-95: Pasting a URL over selected text creates a link.
- [x] ISC-96: Anti: the editor never holds more than one selection range.
- [x] ISC-177: Dragging selected text to another position in the editor moves it.
- [x] ISC-178: A text drag undoes in one step.
- [x] ISC-179: Text drag works in the real app while the window accepts native file drops.
- [x] ISC-190: Anti: a paste never changes a read-only document.
- [x] ISC-191: A drawn block is never a drop target.

### F7 · Workspace
Why: Markdown lives among other files, so the sidebar must manage them and renames must never strand a link.

- [x] ISC-97: Multiple root folders show in the tree.
- [x] ISC-98: A loose file opens without any root folder.
- [x] ISC-99: A 10,000-entry folder renders under 200 rows.
- [x] ISC-100: The tree creates a new file.
- [x] ISC-101: The tree renames a file.
- [x] ISC-102: Dragging in the tree moves a file.
- [x] ISC-103: Deleting moves a file to the Recycle Bin, once the owner has confirmed it (ISC-227).
- [x] ISC-104: Reveal in Explorer selects the file.
- [ ] ISC-105: [DROPPED — see Decisions 2026-11-20]
- [x] ISC-576: A file row in the sidebar shows an icon for its file type (a subset of Material Icon Theme: Markdown, text, JSON, YAML, TOML, scripts, images, archives, lock and config files and more); the type is read from the whole name first, then the longest extension, case-insensitively, and an unknown name gets a default icon; folder rows keep their icons.
- [x] ISC-577: The icons are one local sprite with a symbol for every icon the map returns and nothing else, no network reference and no script, and the licence of the icons ships beside it.
- [x] ISC-578: In `pnpm dev` the tree shows the type icons, readable in Dark, Light and Sepia, none of them on a dark box.
- [ ] ISC-579: The owner finds the file icons right in the tree in all three themes.
- [x] ISC-580: Every file row in the sidebar is drawn the same, whatever its type: no row is dimmed for not being Markdown.
- [x] ISC-581: Code text uses the code font from the settings (the built-in code stack while it is empty): inline code in a paragraph, a heading, a list, a quote or a table cell, and the text of a code block; the body font never reaches it.
- [x] ISC-590: Several rows of the sidebar tree can be selected: Ctrl+click adds a row or takes it out, Shift+click and Shift+Up or Down select the rows from the one that started it, a plain click or Escape goes back to one row, and Ctrl or Shift never opens a file or a folder. The last row added is the lead the keyboard moves from.
- [x] ISC-591: The Delete key and the menu's Delete act on the whole selection with one question: how many files and folders, how many open tabs close and how many files lose unsaved edits across all of them. A row inside a selected folder is not counted twice, a workspace folder is left out with a note, and one row asks as before.
- [x] ISC-592: Dragging a selected row carries the whole selection into a folder, and dragging an unselected row takes only that row. A row that may not go there is skipped, one line says how many moved, and the moved rows are selected where they went.
- [x] ISC-593: With `links.onRename` set to ask, a drag of several rows asks once, says how many more it covers, and applies the answer to all: Yes updates links, No moves plainly, Cancel stops with what was already moved kept.
- [x] ISC-594: The menu of a row inside the selection acts on all of it and offers Delete, and Open All when two or more of the rows are files (ISC-706), and the menu of a row outside it selects that row alone and offers the usual items; F2 does nothing with several rows selected.
- [x] ISC-595: In `pnpm dev` Ctrl+click, Shift+click, Shift+Down, the one Delete question and a drag of three files into a folder work as above.
- [x] ISC-596: The owner selects three files in the real window, deletes them with one question, and drags them into another folder.
- [x] ISC-597: Rust lets a drag out of the window carry only paths inside a workspace root that exist, each once and none inside another one carried, and refuses the whole drag for one path outside a root, for a file opened by hand outside every root, for a path that climbs out with dots, for a missing path and for no path.
- [x] ISC-598: A drop that ends a drag that started in this window is never recorded as a drop from outside, so nothing can copy a file onto itself, and the next real drop is recorded as before.
- [x] ISC-599: When the pointer of a tree drag goes past any edge of the window, the page ends its own drag and hands the dragged rows (the selection, or the pressed row when it is not selected, folders taking their rows along) to the host once; a drag that stays inside the window is unchanged; drops are ignored while the hand-over runs and for 500 ms after; a refusal is said in a toast and the rows stay.
- [x] ISC-600: In a real window, dragging two files and a folder of the workspace onto the Desktop makes copies (the folder whole), the sources stay where they are, Escape cancels with nothing copied, the cursor shows a copy, and dropping them back on the window copies nothing.
- [x] ISC-619: After a restart the sidebar tree opens each root as it was left: a root that was collapsed stays collapsed and an open one opens, and a first start with nothing saved opens each root one level, as before.
- [x] ISC-620: Folders that were open at any depth are open again after a restart, each root keeping its own set, and a folder that is gone is dropped without a message.
- [x] ISC-621: The sidebar comes back shown or hidden as it was left, with the same panel active (Folders, Search, Outline or References); a session without these values shows the sidebar with Folders.
- [x] ISC-622: The owner collapses all roots, opens a few folders deep in others, hides the sidebar, restarts the real window, and finds it as it was left.
- [x] ISC-106: Other text files open as plain source.
- [x] ISC-107: Images open in an image tab.
- [x] ISC-108: The image tab fits a large image to the pane.
- [x] ISC-109: The image tab zooms.
- [x] ISC-110: The image tab pans.
- [x] ISC-111: The image tab shows dimensions and file size.
- [x] ISC-112: Anti: scripts in an SVG never run.
- [x] ISC-113: After a rename, a rescan finds zero links to the old path in the workspace.
- [x] ISC-114: Moving a file updates the relative links inside it.
- [x] ISC-115: Renaming a folder updates links to files within it.
- [x] ISC-116: Link rewrite covers reference definitions.
- [x] ISC-117: Link rewrite covers `<img src>` tags.
- [x] ISC-118: Link rewrite covers percent-encoded targets.
- [x] ISC-119: Link rewrite covers angle-bracket targets.
- [x] ISC-120: Link rewrite preserves `#anchor` suffixes.
- [x] ISC-121: Link rewrite covers folders excluded from the tree. (Since Phase 7.1 it does not enter the built-in excluded folders of ISC-282, which hold build and dependency output; ISC-294 says so for every scan. A folder the tree hides for any other reason is still covered.)
- [x] ISC-122: Link rewrite writes closed files directly.
- [x] ISC-123: Link rewrite gives dirty tabs undoable, unsaved edits.
- [x] ISC-124: Link rewrite covers other text files. (Since 2026-11-11 it covers only Markdown files, by ISC-253.)
- [x] ISC-175: Link rewrite reloads clean open tabs.
- [x] ISC-125: Anti: link rewrite never changes bytes outside link targets.
- [x] ISC-126: A rename shows the number of links and files first. (Since P7.1-T5 the question comes before any file is read, so it has no counts; the counts come after, as a line saying what was updated: ISC-316 and ISC-318.)
- [x] ISC-127: `links.confirmRewrite: false` skips the prompt. (Since P7.1-T5 the setting is `links.onRename`: `update` is the old `false`, `ask` the old `true`, and `never` looks for no links at all; ISC-315 and ISC-316.)
- [x] ISC-128: Files that fail to rewrite are listed.
- [x] ISC-129: Anti: a rewrite scan never pushes typing past 16 ms.
- [x] ISC-130: Ctrl+P finds a file by partial name.
- [x] ISC-131: Ctrl+Shift+P runs a command by name.
- [x] ISC-132: A file created outside mdmate appears in the tree.
- [x] ISC-636: The sidebar cannot be made narrower, by the mouse or the keys, than its tab strip needs: the minimum width is the measured width of the strip's tabs, and a saved width below it is raised to it when the session is restored.
- [x] ISC-637: While the References tab is shown the minimum width includes that tab, and it falls back to the width of the three fixed tabs when References goes away.
- [x] ISC-638: The owner drags the sidebar to its narrowest in the real window, with and without References, in Dark, Light and Sepia, and no tab label leaves the sidebar or touches the pane beside it.
- [x] ISC-645: A setting `workspace.hiddenNames` lists names the tree does not show; it holds `.git`, `.idea`, `.vscode`, `.vs`, `.svn`, `.hg`, `.DS_Store` and `Thumbs.db` by default, takes exact names that match without regard to case, drops blanks and repeats, refuses a value that is not a list of names, and has a Settings row with chips and a reset.
- [x] ISC-646: The tree hides every file and folder whose name is in `workspace.hiddenNames`, at any depth and live when the setting changes, and never hides a root.
- [x] ISC-647: Quick Open, folder search, link scans and the workspace index still see the files and folders that the tree hides by name, and the Rust listing still returns them.
- [x] ISC-648: The owner finds, in the real window, that `.git`, `.idea` and `.vscode` are gone from the tree of a root that holds them, that `.gitignore` and `.github` are still there, and that a name added in Settings disappears at once.
- [x] ISC-662: No symbol of the file-type icon sprite paints a background box: none starts with an unfilled path that covers its whole view box.
- [x] ISC-706: With two or more files selected in the sidebar the context menu has Open All; with one file, or only folders, it has not; selected folders take no part in it.
- [x] ISC-707: Open All opens the selected files in the active pane in tree order, a file already open takes focus without a second tab, the first file is the active tab at the end, and a file that fails reports a toast while the others still open.
- [x] ISC-708: Enter on a selection of two or more files does what Open All does; Enter on one row is unchanged.
- [ ] ISC-709: The owner opens several selected files from the menu and with Enter in the real window and finds them in the active pane, the first one active.

### F8 · Search and outline
Why: long documents and folders are only usable when you can jump straight to the right place.

- [x] ISC-133: Ctrl+F finds text in the file.
- [x] ISC-134: Ctrl+H replaces text in the file.
- [x] ISC-135: Find supports regular expressions.
- [x] ISC-136: Find supports case-sensitive matching.
- [x] ISC-137: Ctrl+Shift+F searches every root folder.
- [x] ISC-138: Anti: a folder search never pushes typing past 16 ms.
- [x] ISC-139: Clicking a search result opens the file at the match.
- [x] ISC-140: Starting a new search stops the previous one.
- [x] ISC-141: The outline panel lists the file's headings.
- [x] ISC-142: Clicking an outline heading jumps there.
- [x] ISC-143: The outline reaches the last heading of the 10 MB fixture.
- [x] ISC-144: The outline reflects unsaved heading edits.
- [x] ISC-605: Space on the selected outline heading scrolls the editor to it and leaves focus in the outline, Enter jumps and moves focus to the editor, and the arrow keys only move the selection.
- [x] ISC-606: Outline headings are indented by their rank among the levels the document uses, so the top level has no extra indent, with a larger step than before.
- [x] ISC-607: Outline headings of levels 1, 2 and 3 have three different sizes and levels 4 to 6 take the level 3 size. (The bold rule that this claim held moved to ISC-639 and ISC-640 on 2026-11-21.)
- [x] ISC-608: Outline rows are taller than the tree's, from their own height token (about 30 px) that the virtual list uses, and deeper ranks are progressively more muted while the text keeps its contrast.
- [x] ISC-609: The outline marks the row of the section that holds the caret, follows the caret and heading edits, and marks nothing above the first heading.
- [x] ISC-610: The owner finds the outline layout right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-639: The lowest heading level a document uses is drawn bold in the outline, also when it is the only level the document uses.
- [x] ISC-640: When the lowest level occurs once, the next level the document uses is bold too (one H1 makes H2 bold; one H2 and no H1 makes H3 bold); the step does not chain, and a lowest level that occurs more than once does not make the next level bold.

### F9 · Configuration and themes
Why: the look must be yours to change without rebuilding, which is why everything runs on tokens.

- [x] ISC-145: Dark and light built-in themes switch live.
- [x] ISC-146: Anti: built-in themes copy no SublimeText assets.
- [x] ISC-147: Saving `settings.json` applies changes without a restart.
- [x] ISC-148: Invalid settings keep the last good values with a notice.
- [x] ISC-149: Saving `keybindings.json` changes a shortcut.
- [x] ISC-150: A user theme file overrides a token.
- [x] ISC-151: Saving a theme file updates the app live.
- [x] ISC-152: The portable marker keeps data beside the exe.
- [x] ISC-153: Common toggles run from the command palette.
- [x] ISC-343: A second Open Settings focuses the one Settings tab and opens no second.
- [x] ISC-344: A Settings tab comes back after a restart in the same pane, at the same category.
- [x] ISC-345: Splitting the pane of the Settings tab opens no second Settings tab.
- [x] ISC-346: `Mod+,` opens Settings.
- [x] ISC-347: The title bar menu lists Open Settings but not Open settings.json, while the command palette still lists and runs both.
- [x] ISC-348: A gear button opens Settings.
- [x] ISC-349: Changing a control writes only that key to `settings.json` and keeps every other key, unknown ones included.
- [x] ISC-350: A control set back to its default removes its key from `settings.json`.
- [x] ISC-351: Saving `settings.json` by hand updates the open Settings screen with no reload.
- [x] ISC-352: A `settings.json` that is not valid JSON keeps the last good values and the screen shows a banner naming the line.
- [x] ISC-353: A value of the wrong type or outside its range is ignored, flagged on its row, and the last good value stays in use.
- [x] ISC-354: With unsaved edits in the `settings.json` tab, changing a control asks Save, Discard or Cancel before anything is written.
- [x] ISC-355: Every setting row shows its label, a one-line description and its JSON key, and the modified dot and reset icon only while the value differs from the default.
- [x] ISC-356: Reset on a row removes its key and applies the default.
- [x] ISC-357: The search box shows only the rows whose label, description or key match, across all categories.
- [x] ISC-358: The Settings screen reads well in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-359: `editor.wordWrap` set to false stops the editor wrapping long lines, with no restart.
- [x] ISC-360: A font family set in `fonts.heading`, `fonts.body` or `fonts.code` is used for that kind of text only.
- [x] ISC-361: An empty `fonts.*` family uses the built-in stack.
- [x] ISC-362: `fonts.size` sets the body size and each heading level follows `fonts.headingScale`.
- [x] ISC-363: `fonts.codeSize` sets the size of code blocks; inline code does not read it (refined 2026-11-20, see Decisions).
- [x] ISC-364: `files.largeFileMB` sets the size above which the next opened file uses plain-text mode.
- [x] ISC-365: `recent.files` and `recent.folders` cap the two recent lists.
- [x] ISC-366: A changed `workspace.excludedFolders` re-indexes the open folders and search skips a newly added name.
- [x] ISC-367: Reset to built-in list restores the built-in excluded folders (21 today).
- [x] ISC-368: An absolute or escaping `images.pasteFolder` is refused on its row and the last good folder stays in use for the next paste.
- [x] ISC-369: While `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` is set, the Links row is read-only and names the variable.
- [x] ISC-370: Toggle Word Wrap and Toggle Developer Tools show their current on or off state in the palette and the menu.
- [x] ISC-371: Switch Theme lists Dark, Light, Sepia, System and every user theme file, and choosing one applies it.
- [x] ISC-372: The Keyboard category lists every command with its current shortcut.
- [x] ISC-373: Pressing a new combination on a Keyboard row rebinds that command at once and writes `keybindings.json`.
- [x] ISC-374: A combination already used names the other command, and Replace moves it to this one.
- [x] ISC-375: Reset on a Keyboard row removes its override.
- [x] ISC-376: A `keybindings.json` with an invalid entry keeps the default for that command and shows a notice.
- [x] ISC-377: The Sepia built-in theme switches live.
- [x] ISC-378: Sepia declares the same tokens as Dark and passes every contrast pair of the theme test.
- [x] ISC-379: The System theme follows the operating system and switches live when it changes.
- [x] ISC-380: Customize copies the selected theme into the themes folder and selects the copy.
- [x] ISC-381: The colour editor lists every token of the theme in groups.
- [x] ISC-382: Changing a colour in the editor writes the theme file and updates the app at once.
- [x] ISC-383: A colour that brings a required pair under its contrast floor shows a warning beside it.
- [x] ISC-384: Anti: `settings.json` and `keybindings.json` are read only from the app data folder, never from an opened folder.
- [x] ISC-385: Anti: Customize and the colour editor never write to a built-in theme.
- [x] ISC-386: A write to `settings.json` that fails, or meets a file that is not valid JSON, leaves the file and the live value as they were and says why.
- [x] ISC-387: Anti: A saved tab of a kind this version does not know is dropped when a session is restored, and its path is never opened as a file.
- [x] ISC-388: A saved session that names more than one Settings tab restores the first and drops the rest.
- [x] ISC-389: Anti: The Settings view (`SettingsView.vue`, `SettingRow.vue`) is loaded with `import()` on first use, never statically.
- [x] ISC-390: A switch on the Settings screen shows a track and a thumb in the on and the off state.
- [x] ISC-391: A change made on the Settings screen shows at once in an open `settings.json` tab that holds no unsaved edits.
- [x] ISC-392: Font settings change the document text only: the title bar, menus, tabs, sidebar and the Settings screen keep their fonts.
- [x] ISC-393: Each font family field shows a sample line in the family typed, before the value is accepted.
- [x] ISC-394: A toggle run from the palette or the menu while the settings.json tab has unsaved edits shows the Settings screen's question and writes nothing until it is answered.
- [x] ISC-395: Switch Theme opens a list of the themes that exist now, marks the one in use, and choosing one writes it; ISC-371 widens the list to Sepia, System and user files with the themes (P8-T3).
- [x] ISC-396: Light is a choice, and a theme is applied: Light, Dark or a name this build does not know (it shows Dark) puts exactly one of `dark` and `light` on the page root, from the file, the Settings screen or Switch Theme, and the window looks like it.
- [x] ISC-397: A file that is not Markdown shows syntax colors, by its extension, when the extension is in `editor.highlightExtensions`; changing the setting or renaming the file changes this in the open tab with no reopening.
- [x] ISC-398: The Syntax colors row shows one tag per language (A to Z) with the language's extensions on hover; choosing a language in the picker adds all its extensions and removing its tag removes them; an extension no language owns shows as a bare `.ext` tag.
- [x] ISC-399: The settings search field is at the top of the left list, above Appearance.
- [x] ISC-400: A tooltip and the hint drawn inside the editor have their own surface in both themes (`--tooltip` and `--tooltip-foreground`), readable and, in Dark, lighter than the page.
- [x] ISC-401: A focused editor draws no outline of its own.
- [x] ISC-402: A rebinding works inside an open editor, including onto a key CodeMirror's own keymap uses: the command runs and the editor's own key does not.
- [x] ISC-403: Overrides in `keybindings.json` are resolved against the final set of bindings, whatever order the keys are in: a swap of two commands' shortcuts works; two overrides on one shortcut both fall back to their defaults with a notice naming both commands; an override onto a shortcut another command still holds by default falls back with a notice.
- [x] ISC-404: A `null` entry in `keybindings.json` unbinds the command: it has no shortcut, its old key does nothing, and the key can be given to another command.
- [x] ISC-405: A change on the Keyboard category while the `keybindings.json` tab holds unsaved edits asks Save, Don't save or Cancel before anything is written, like ISC-354.
- [x] ISC-406: Recording a shortcut: the next combination is taken; Escape cancels and changes nothing; a modifier pressed alone is ignored; a combination with neither Mod nor Alt (and not a function key) is refused with the reason on the row; none of the pressed keys runs a command or reaches the editor or the palette.
- [x] ISC-407: The shortcut an event records is matched by that same event, for Shift or Alt with a digit, function keys, Space and Cmd on a Mac.
- [x] ISC-408: Reset all on the Keyboard category writes `{}` to `keybindings.json` and every command is on its default shortcut.
- [x] ISC-409: Advanced has an Open keybindings.json row, and the palette an Open keybindings.json command, that open the file as a tab, making it when it is missing.
- [x] ISC-410: A `keybindings.json` that is not one JSON object keeps the last good shortcuts and shows a banner on the Keyboard category naming the line.
- [x] ISC-411: After a rebind, the shortcut shown by the Keyboard row, the palette and the menu is the new one, with no reload.
- [x] ISC-412: Every command names exactly one of six groups (File, Find, Formatting, View, Tabs & Navigation, App), and the 41 built-in commands fall 10, 5, 7, 7, 6 and 6 as the owner agreed.
- [x] ISC-413: Registering a command with a group outside the six is refused with an error that names the command.
- [x] ISC-414: The Keyboard category shows each group's heading in the fixed order, with that group's commands A to Z under it and every command once.
- [x] ISC-415: While a search is typed, a group with no matching command has no heading, and the groups that stay keep their order.
- [x] ISC-416: The search field on the Keyboard title row matches a command by the words of its title, id, group name or shortcut; a word holding `+` must equal whole `+`-separated parts of one shortcut, so `ctrl+s` finds Save and Save As and not Close All.
- [x] ISC-417: A search that matches nothing says "No shortcut matches that.", and Escape in the field clears it.
- [x] ISC-418: Moving focus from a recording row into the search field ends the recording and changes nothing in the file.
- [x] ISC-419: The search field sits beside the heading, which still reads only "Keyboard", and the global Search settings box still lists no shortcut.

- [x] ISC-421: Reset all on the Keyboard page removes every entry of `keybindings.json`, unknown ids included, and leaves the comments.

- [x] ISC-423: Comments and trailing commas are read without a banner, and a real syntax error still shows the banner with its line and column.
- [x] ISC-424: Open settings.json and Open keybindings.json write the commented template into a missing, empty or `{}` file before opening it, and never replace a file that holds anything else or a tab with unsaved edits.

- [x] ISC-426: Removing the `//` from one template line and saving makes that setting or shortcut take effect.

- [x] ISC-428: The restore never writes an example that is already in the file and never writes one for a key the template does not know.
- [x] ISC-429: The first change made from the screen to a missing, empty or `{}` file starts from the template, so the file is never a bare list of entries, and a change that changes nothing writes nothing.
- [x] ISC-431: Taking an entry out restores its commented example only in a file that carries the template (its header comment); a file written by hand or before the template existed gets no commented fragment.
- [x] ISC-432: On the Keyboard page a group heading is larger than a row name and has more space above it than before (at least 32px above every heading after the first).
- [x] ISC-433: The Keyboard page has no Reset all button; the Advanced page has "Reset all shortcuts" beside Open keybindings.json and "Reset all settings" beside Open settings.json.
- [x] ISC-434: Neither reset changes a file until its confirmation dialog is confirmed; Cancel and Escape leave the file as it was, and the dialog opens with Cancel focused.
- [x] ISC-435: Confirming "Reset all settings" takes every entry out of settings.json and keeps its comments (the template comes back when the file started as the template); both reset buttons are off while their file holds no entry.
- [x] ISC-436: A reset asked for while the file's tab has unsaved edits shows the same save-first question on the Advanced page, and writes nothing until it is answered.
- [x] ISC-437: Both themes define a --warning token, mapped to the text-warning utility, with contrast of at least 4.5 on --background (amber in Dark, a darker amber in Light).
- [x] ISC-438: The "is used by" message shown when a recorded shortcut is already taken uses the warning color, not the plain text color.
- [x] ISC-439: With `editor.limitContentWidth` on, the text column of an editor tab is at most `editor.contentMaxWidth` px wide, not counting the padding round it, and is centered in the pane; in a pane narrower than that it uses the whole width. While word wrap is off a long line runs past the column and the limit steps aside.
- [x] ISC-440: With `editor.limitContentWidth` off the text column uses the whole width of the pane, as before.
- [x] ISC-441: A change of `editor.limitContentWidth` or `editor.contentMaxWidth`, from the screen or by hand in the file, reaches the open tabs at once, with no restart and no reopening.
- [x] ISC-442: The `settings.json` and `keybindings.json` tabs keep the whole width whatever the two settings say, and so does the Settings screen, which is not an editor; the two JSON tabs are found by file name, in any folder.
- [x] ISC-443: Before the file is read, or when it cannot be, the editor already limits the column to the default width: the CSS fallback equals the defaults.
- [x] ISC-444: In the settings.json and keybindings.json rows of the Advanced page, Reset all comes before the Open button, both Reset all buttons are one width and both Open buttons are one width.

- [x] ISC-446: The Sepia theme takes MarkdownWeave's cream page (`#fdf6e3`) and brown text (`#3b2f1e`) and has a value of its own for every token.
- [x] ISC-447: The root names the palette in use in `data-theme` and carries the `dark` class only when the theme is dark, so Sepia and any theme based on a light palette never switch on Tailwind's `dark:` variants.
- [x] ISC-448: A user theme file is data: a bad value, an unknown key, a file that is not JSON and a selected name with no file each fall back (to the base theme's value, to the base theme, to Dark) with a notice that says why, and the app keeps working.
- [x] ISC-449: Anti: a value in a theme file reaches the page's styles only if it is `#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa` or `oklch()` with plain numbers; nothing else, not even a valid CSS color, is passed on.
- [x] ISC-450: Anti: the themes folder is the only place theme files are read or written; a name is a plain name with the fixed `.json` extension, a directory, a link, an over-size file or a file past the count cap is skipped, and a create never replaces a file.
- [x] ISC-451: A theme file named like a built-in (`dark`, `light`, `sepia`, `system`, any case) is ignored with a notice and the built-in keeps its name.
- [x] ISC-452: Editing, adding or deleting a file in `themes/` while the app runs reaches the screen and the Theme list with no restart; deleting the selected theme shows Dark with a notice and leaves the setting alone, and bringing the file back restores it.
- [x] ISC-453: Customize writes one new file named `<Theme> copy` (then `copy 2`, and so on) that holds the base and a literal color for every token of what is shown now (System's current Dark or Light, a user theme's own colors), never replaces a file, selects the copy, and leaves the built-in CSS alone.
- [x] ISC-454: The colour editor changes only the token the user touched, keeping every comment, unknown key and the layout of the file; it reads the file fresh before each write, refuses to write over a file that is not valid JSON, and coalesces a picker drag into a few writes with the last value kept.
- [x] ISC-455: A token's text field takes `#rrggbb`-style and `oklch()` values, shows the reason for a bad one and writes nothing; a token whose value has alpha has a color picker and an opacity field in its popover, because the native picker held no alpha (ISC-492).
- [x] ISC-456: With "Hide contrast warnings" off (ISC-465), a colour that brings a required pair under its contrast floor shows a warning with the pair, the ratio and the floor, and is still written; the warning goes when the pair passes again.

- [x] ISC-459: The data folder is `<exe dir>/data` when a file named `mdmate.portable` sits beside the exe, the usual app-data folder when it does not, and `MDMATE_DATA_DIR` wins over both; a directory of that name is no marker, a marker anywhere else is ignored.
- [x] ISC-460: In portable mode every kind of data the app keeps (settings, keybindings, themes, session, recent list, recovered buffers) is written under `<exe dir>/data` and nowhere else.
- [x] ISC-461: Anti: an exe path that cannot be read never stops the app or panics the data folder lookup; the usual folder is used.
- [x] ISC-462: Every sub-group heading on the Settings screen (Theme, Fonts, Window, Theme colors, the color groups Core, Sidebar, Chrome, Editor and Syntax, and the Keyboard groups) is drawn in the theme's `--primary`; a page title stays in the text color.
- [x] ISC-463: Anti: one heading component draws every sub-group heading, and none sets a text color of its own.
- [x] ISC-464: The Appearance rows sit under the headings Theme, Fonts, Window and Theme colors; a search shows the rows with no headings.

- [x] ISC-532: A Settings category on the --accent background uses --accent-foreground for its text, current, hovered and focused.
- [x] ISC-533: In the real window the current, hovered and focused Settings category show --accent-foreground on --accent.
- [x] ISC-534: Every fill with a color token sets that token's partner text color at the same state, in the app's own code.
- [x] ISC-535: The fills that carry no text are listed with a reason, and a stale entry fails the test.
- [x] ISC-536: In the real window the pairs show as the theme gives them, in a loud custom theme, Sepia and Light.
- [x] ISC-537: `--destructive-foreground` is a theme token in the editor list, the mapping, all three built-in themes and the contrast pairs.
- [x] ISC-538: Every `--destructive` fill sets `--destructive-foreground`, and the pair scan enforces it.
- [x] ISC-539: In the real window the close button and the error bubble show --destructive-foreground on --destructive.
- [x] ISC-540: Hovering a color token in the editor explains where the app uses it.
- [x] ISC-541: A theme's base is shown by name only; it cannot be changed from the editor.
- [x] ISC-542: In the real window the explanations show on hover, and the base reads as a name.
- [x] ISC-543: The error bubble under a name box in the file tree is always fully inside the sidebar: it is anchored to the row, capped at the row width, and wraps.
- [x] ISC-544: The drop preview of a dragged tab has no border. (Its short transition was taken back: ISC-548.)
- [x] ISC-549: A command in the View group, "Toggle Limit Content Width", flips the Limit content width setting, shows its state, and is in the command palette.
- [x] ISC-550: The command has the default key Alt+Shift+W, which no other command has, and the Keyboard category lists and finds it so it can be rebound.
- [x] ISC-552: Turning the content width limit on also turns word wrap on, in the same write, from the command and from the Settings screen; turning it off or changing anything else leaves word wrap alone.
- [x] ISC-553: In the real window the width command and the Settings switch make the column appear with word wrap off before, and the word wrap controls read On.
- [x] ISC-557: The zoom has levels from 50 to 300 percent in steps of 10, and a step from a level off the grid goes to the next multiple of 10, so no step is lost.
- [x] ISC-558: Ctrl+=, Ctrl+Shift+= and the numpad plus zoom the window in, Ctrl+- zooms it out and Ctrl+0 resets it to 100, as the commands Zoom In, Zoom Out and Reset Zoom, which the menu, the palette and the Keyboard category list.
- [x] ISC-559: Ctrl with the mouse wheel zooms the window a step in or out, and the wheel without Ctrl scrolls as before.
- [x] ISC-560: The `view.zoom` setting holds the level, takes whole levels from 50 to 300, reaches the web view at start and on every change, and has the row Zoom under the heading Window.
- [x] ISC-561: In the real window the zoom scales the whole window, a click, a tab drag and the wheel keep working at 150 percent, and the app starts at the level it was left at.
- [x] ISC-563: Inline code, in text and in a table cell, is drawn in the code family at the size of the text around it, and no rule but the code block's reads the code size.
- [x] ISC-564: With the code size at 18 on a 15 px body, inline code in a paragraph is 15 px, a code block is 18 px, and inline code in a heading or a cell follows that text.
- [x] ISC-551: In the real window the palette entry and the key switch the text column between a centered column and the full width.
- [x] ISC-548: The drop preview carries no transition or animation: it jumps from one target to the next.
- [x] ISC-545: In the real window the error bubble is fully visible in a narrow sidebar and the drop preview glides without a border.
- [x] ISC-546: With more than one pane, only the focused pane shows scrollbar thumbs; another pane shows them while the pointer is over its content, and the Theme preview always shows them.
- [x] ISC-547: In the real window the thumbs follow the focused pane and the pointer, and the Theme preview keeps its thumb.
- [x] ISC-430: A comment in a `.json` or `.jsonc` file is shown in the comment color, in italics, with the strings and numbers inside it in that color too, and a `//` inside a string is not taken for a comment.
- [x] ISC-612: A setting `editor.lineNumbers`, off by default, shows a line-number gutter in every editor; the settings file, its Settings row and a palette command (no default shortcut) change it live and it is kept across restarts.
- [x] ISC-613: The gutter is shown for Markdown, plain text and JSON tabs; when the text is a centred column it sits at the left edge of the column, and otherwise at the left of the pane.
- [x] ISC-614: Line numbers count the frontmatter lines, so the first body line after a 5-line frontmatter is 6; the collapsed frontmatter pill shows no number, and with the caret in it the lines show numbers 1 to 5.
- [x] ISC-615: A wrapped line shows its number once, on its first row; a table or image block shows the number of its first source line and the lines it hides are skipped; the number of the line with the caret is marked.
- [x] ISC-616: The owner finds the line numbers right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia, with a frontmatter, a table and a wrapped line.
- [x] ISC-625: Settings `fonts.lineHeight` and `fonts.codeLineHeight` exist, hold the line heights of today by default, take a unitless number from 1 to 3 in steps of 0.05, and refuse anything else.
- [x] ISC-626: Body text, lists, quotes, tables and every heading level take their line height from `fonts.lineHeight`, live; no heading has a line height of its own.
- [x] ISC-627: Code blocks take their line height from `fonts.codeLineHeight`, live, and the body and inline code are unchanged by it.
- [x] ISC-628: In Settings the text size and its line height share one row, and the code size and its line height share one row, as two short fields with a gap, each wide enough for two digits, each with its own reset; the heading sizes row is unchanged.
- [x] ISC-629: The owner changes both line heights in the real window and finds the body and the code blocks follow them, in Dark, Light and Sepia, with the defaults looking as they did before.
- [x] ISC-661: The line numbers of the gutter sit on the lines they number at any body line height: the gutter's line height is the body line height times the body size.
- [x] ISC-630: Two new theme tokens, the line-number gutter background and its numbers, exist in Dark, Light and Sepia and in the theme editor with their usage text, and a custom theme that leaves them out takes those of its base theme.
- [x] ISC-631: The line-number colour differs from the body text colour and reads at 4.5:1 or more on the gutter background, in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-632: The gutter is painted with its own background token, which equals the page background in Dark, Light and Sepia, its numbers use the code font family at 0.9 times the code size, and the number of the line with the caret keeps the foreground colour.
- [ ] ISC-633: The owner finds the look of the line numbers right in the real window in Dark, Light and Sepia: small, muted, readable, on the page background, with the same room each side of the digits.
- [x] ISC-641: A command lists the installed font families, each once, sorted, with no empty name, and the page can ask for it (the browser mock answers with a short fixed list).
- [x] ISC-642: The heading, body and code font fields are searchable comboboxes: typing filters the families, each option is drawn in its own family, "Built-in" comes first and clears the setting, and a typed name that is not in the list can be used.
- [x] ISC-643: A saved value that is a font stack (`Inter, sans-serif`) or names a family that is not installed is shown as it is and is kept when the Settings page opens and closes.
- [x] ISC-644: The owner picks a font for each of the three fields in the real window and finds the list searchable, the options drawn in their own fonts, and the editor following the choice live.
- [x] ISC-665: Opening the list of a font field shows the option that is the field's value scrolled into view, also when that family is past the first 60.
- [x] ISC-669: The line-number gutter has the same room on the right of the digits as on the left, 10px each, inside the gutter.
- [x] ISC-672: Setting `editor.stripedTables` exists, is a switch that is on by default, is validated, sits in the Editor category, and changes the open editors live.
- [x] ISC-673: A theme token for the stripe colour of tables exists in Dark, Light and Sepia and in the theme editor's editor group with usage text, and the text colour reads at 4.5:1 or more on it. The stripe is plainly visible against the page, a contrast of 1.08 or more, in all three.
- [x] ISC-682: The default of `editor.contentMaxWidth` is 1024.
- [x] ISC-683: The checkbox of a task item is 1em wide and high and sits so its middle is on the middle of a capital letter of the line, not on the x-height.
- [ ] ISC-684: The owner finds the task boxes centered on the text of their line and big enough, in Dark, Light and Sepia, in a proportional and a monospace font.
- [x] ISC-685: The Git program field is right-aligned, and when it is not being edited it is scrolled to the end of its text, so the file name of a long path shows.
- [ ] ISC-686: The owner finds the end of a long Git program path showing in the real window.
- [x] ISC-687: The line number of a heading line is centered on the first row of the heading, a heading being taller than body text and having top padding.
- [x] ISC-688: The owner finds the line number of each heading centered on its heading in the real window, in Dark, Light and Sepia.
- [x] ISC-689: The editors of documents draw no selection or cursor layer of their own, and the theme preview does.
- [x] ISC-690: The owner finds selections drawn only where there are characters, as in Obsidian, in the real window.
- [x] ISC-693: The code background token (`syntax-code-background`) differs from the page background by a contrast ratio of 1.08 or more in Dark, Light and Sepia, so a code block and an inline code span can be told from the page.
- [x] ISC-696: The warning icon of a toast reads the `--warning` colour and no heading token, so it keeps its colour when headings turn neutral.

### F10 · Image authoring
Why: screenshots are the most common thing dropped into documentation, and adding one should take a single paste.

- [x] ISC-154: A pasted image saves to an images folder next to the document (`assets`, created on the first paste), as `image-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.png` (`.png` for a screenshot; a copied JPEG, GIF or WebP keeps the extension of its own type). A picture that has a name of its own (a dropped file) keeps that name instead (ISC-336).
- [x] ISC-155: A pasted image inserts a relative link.
- [x] ISC-156: A dropped image inserts a link at the drop point.
- [x] ISC-157: A drag handle on a selected, loaded image (block or inline, local or `https`) resizes it: width only, aspect ratio kept, from 24 px up to the editor content width.
- [x] ISC-158: A resized Markdown image `![alt](src)` is written as `<img src alt width>`; a resized `<img>` has its `width` set.
- [x] ISC-159: Anti: a resize changes bytes only inside that image's source (and, for an `<img>` with a `height`, only that attribute besides `width`), with one stated exception: a Markdown image alone on the first line of a paragraph that goes on gets one line break after its new tag, so the next line does not become part of the tag's HTML block.
- [x] ISC-160: Unresized images stay standard `![]()` syntax.
- [x] ISC-322: A click on an image selects it (outline and resize handle) and its source stays hidden; Escape or a click elsewhere deselects, and Enter or an arrow key into it shows its source.
- [x] ISC-323: An `<img>` tag with `src`, `alt`, `title`, `width` and `height` draws as an image through the same address allowlist as `![]()` (ISC-180); a tag with any other attribute shows its allowed ones only, and other HTML stays source.
- [x] ISC-324: Anti: an `<img>` with a refused address, a `style`, or an `on*` attribute loads nothing from that address and runs no script.
- [x] ISC-325: Delete or Backspace on a selected image removes its whole source, as one undo step.
- [x] ISC-326: Resizing an `<img>` that has a `height` removes that `height` and changes nothing else in the tag.
- [x] ISC-327: A paste or a drop-copy into an untitled document saves the image in that document's own draft folder (`assets/image-….png` under it) and inserts the normal relative link, which draws the image.
- [x] ISC-328: An image dropped on document text links in place when the file is inside the document's workspace folder, and is copied into the images folder and linked there when it is outside.
- [x] ISC-329: Anti: a pasted or copied image never overwrites an existing file; a taken name that holds another picture gets `-2`, `-3`.
- [x] ISC-330: `settings.json` holds `images.pasteFolder` (default `assets`) and a saved change applies to the next paste with no restart.
- [x] ISC-331: The first Save As of an untitled document copies its draft images into the images folder beside the saved file and then removes the draft folder; the links already resolve there, and a name that is taken there gets `-2`, `-3` with its link updated.
- [x] ISC-332: Closing an untitled tab removes its draft folder once the close is final (after "Don't Save", or at once for a tab with no changes), by every route that closes a tab; quitting the app keeps it, since the session restores the tab. Anti: a draft folder an open or restorable tab holds is never removed, and a folder whose tab is gone (a failed removal, a crash) is removed at the next start-up.
- [x] ISC-333: Anti: an image link in an untitled document that leaves its own draft folder, or points into another draft folder, never loads; the asset protocol is granted one draft folder at a time and never the folder that holds them all.
- [x] ISC-334: Anti: a picture whose source text an edit has deleted (an undo of a paste, with the caret elsewhere) is never left drawn.
- [x] ISC-335: A picture pasted, dropped or carried by a first Save As whose bytes are exactly those of a file already in the destination images folder (the document's `assets`, or its draft folder), whatever that file is called, is not written again: the link goes to the file that is there, with no notice. Dropping one picture ten times leaves one file.
- [x] ISC-336: A dropped file's copy keeps the file's own name (its extension is the type of its bytes). A character Windows forbids in a name, and `#`, `%` and `&`, become `-`; a trailing dot or space too; the name is cut to 100 characters, a name Windows keeps for a device (`con`, `nul`, `com1`) gets `image-` before it, and spaces, dots and non-ASCII letters stay. A file with no name left is named like a paste. Another picture with the name that is taken gets `-2`, `-3`.
- [x] ISC-337: Anti: reusing a file never removes, rewrites or lists for removal a file the call did not make: a Save As that fails after reusing leaves that file as it was, and a link is only pointed at a reused name that needs no `<...>` unless it already was that name.
- [x] ISC-338: Everything the app tells the user went wrong or was refused (a failed open, a link whose target or heading is missing, a refused drop or move, a restore notice) shows as a toast, a warning for a refusal and an error for a failure, and the status bar shows only the find count and the indexing note.
- [x] ISC-339: A toast closes itself 12 seconds after it showed, on its own time; it waits while the pointer is over the stack; the same message shown again is one toast whose time starts again; different messages stack, four at a time, in the bottom right corner, with a close button at its top right.
- [x] ISC-340: Every toast closes when the active tab of the focused pane changes (a switch, another pane taking the focus, or the tab closing), and none closes while it stays the same.
- [x] ISC-341: Each dropped file that is refused or fails gets a toast of its own that starts with its name; a file that is not a picture, an SVG or too large is a warning, a failure of the disk an error.
- [x] ISC-342: In the real window the toasts read well in both themes, with an error and a warning clearly different, and none is left behind on another tab.

### F11 · Release
Why: it only becomes your everyday Markdown app once it opens from a double-click and strangers can install it.

- [ ] ISC-161: A second launch with a file opens a tab in the first instance.
- [ ] ISC-162: A relative path resolves against the second launch's working directory.
- [ ] ISC-163: `mdmate file.md` opens the file on a cold launch.
- [ ] ISC-164: The installer's opt-in sets the `.md` association.
- [ ] ISC-165: Anti: the installer changes no association without the opt-in.
- [ ] ISC-166: `pnpm tauri build` produces an NSIS installer.
- [ ] ISC-167: The portable zip runs from any folder.
- [ ] ISC-168: A version tag publishes a GitHub Release with both artifacts.
- [x] ISC-169: The repo carries an MIT `LICENSE`.
- [ ] ISC-170: The README lets a stranger install and use mdmate.

### F12 · File lifecycle
Why: an everyday editor starts, keeps and manages files without sending you to Explorer, and files arrive by drag as often as by dialog.

- [x] ISC-192: Open Folder adds the chosen folder as a workspace root.
- [x] ISC-193: Removing a root takes it out of the tree and revokes its access.
- [x] ISC-194: Workspace roots are restored on restart.
- [x] ISC-195: A folder dropped from Explorer outside the tree becomes a workspace root.
- [x] ISC-196: The tree creates a new folder.
- [x] ISC-197: Files dropped from Explorer on a tree folder are copied into it.
- [x] ISC-198: A folder dropped from Explorer on a tree folder is copied whole.
- [x] ISC-199: A name clash on a copy offers Replace, Keep both and Skip.
- [x] ISC-200: Markdown and text files dropped from Explorer open in tabs in the pane they land on.
- [x] ISC-201: An image dropped from Explorer outside a document's text opens in an image tab.
- [x] ISC-202: Anti: a path that no native drop delivered is never opened or copied.
- [x] ISC-203: Ctrl+N opens an untitled tab.
- [x] ISC-204: The first save of an untitled tab writes UTF-8 with LF to the path chosen in the save dialog.
- [x] ISC-205: The save dialog suggests a file name from the first heading.
- [x] ISC-206: Unsaved untitled tabs survive a restart.
- [x] ISC-207: Save As writes to the chosen path and the tab follows the new file.
- [x] ISC-208: Anti: Save As never changes the original file.
- [x] ISC-209: Save As on a read-only document writes UTF-8 and the tab becomes editable.
- [x] ISC-210: Revert File discards unsaved edits and reloads the file after a confirm.
- [x] ISC-211: Close All closes every tab, asking once for each dirty document (of its own pane only, see ISC-307).
- [x] ISC-212: Open Recent lists the last 10 files and 5 folders across restarts.
- [x] ISC-213: Close Others closes every other tab in the pane.
- [x] ISC-214: Copy Path copies the file's absolute path.
- [x] ISC-215: Copy Relative Path copies the path from its root, or the absolute path outside every root.
- [x] ISC-216: Reveal in Sidebar selects the file in the tree.
- [x] ISC-217: F2 renames the active file through the link-rewrite flow.
- [x] ISC-218: Find File References lists every file that links to the active file.
- [x] ISC-219: Anti: renaming or moving a file, or a folder holding open files, in the tree never leaves an open document on the old path; its tab, its saves and its disk watch follow the file.
- [x] ISC-220: Rename in the tab menu is off for a file outside every open folder, and a hover shows a themed shadcn tooltip (never a native title) that says why.
- [x] ISC-221: Shift+F12 runs Find File References for the active file.
- [x] ISC-222: Anti: no tooltip in the app is a native `title` attribute; every tooltip is the themed shadcn Tooltip.
- [x] ISC-223: Anti: a right click on a tab never fails to open the tab menu, with or without a tooltip showing on that tab.
- [x] ISC-224: A tab shows its tooltip only when its name is cut off, and a tab, a tree file and a tree folder wait 1 s before showing one; every other hint keeps 300 ms.
- [x] ISC-225: Anti: the tooltip arrow never draws over the tooltip box.
- [x] ISC-226: Anti: no tooltip drawn inside the editor is a native `title` attribute; a broken link, the frontmatter pill and an image that did not load show a themed tooltip that follows dark and light.
- [x] ISC-227: Deleting a file or a folder from the tree, by the menu or the Delete key, asks first in a themed shadcn dialog, and nothing is deleted until the owner confirms.
- [x] ISC-228: Confirming a delete closes every tab of the deleted file, or under the deleted folder, even one with unsaved edits; the dialog says how many tabs close and whether any is unsaved.
- [x] ISC-229: The tree menu has Copy Path and Copy Relative Path for a file, a folder and (path only) a workspace folder, and Find File References for a file.
- [x] ISC-230: The reveal item reads Open Containing Folder for a file (everywhere, tab menu included) and Open Folder for a folder, and Open Folder opens that folder itself.
- [x] ISC-231: The sidebar is resized by dragging its right edge between 12rem and half the window; hiding and showing it, and a restart, keep the width; a double click resets it.
- [x] ISC-232: An image whose relative path climbs out of the document folder but stays inside the workspace folder loads; a path out of the workspace folder (or out of the folder of a document in no workspace folder) never loads.
- [x] ISC-233: An image tab keeps its zoom and pan while the owner works in other tabs, and a tab never zoomed still fits.
- [x] ISC-234: Every item of the tab menu and the tree menu whose action has a key shows that key (Close and Close All, Rename, Delete, Find File References), and the two menus call the same action by the same name.
- [x] ISC-235: Anti: a tooltip, an editor hint or the delete dialog never splits a word that fits on a line by itself; the whole word moves to the next line, and only a word longer than the line (a path) is cut.
- [x] ISC-236: Anti: the status bar never shows the operating system name; it holds a message or the find match count and nothing else (the shown file's path or name is in the title bar).
- [x] ISC-237: The key shown at the right of a menu item sits on the same text baseline as the item label, with the font sizes unchanged (label 14 px, key 12 px).
- [x] ISC-238: The frontmatter pill says only "Frontmatter"; it never lists the block's field names.
- [x] ISC-239: While Ctrl (Cmd) is held and the pointer is over a link that Ctrl+click follows, the cursor is a pointer; over plain text, a missing link, a table-cell link or with the keys let go, it is not.
- [x] ISC-240: While a tree row is dragged the cursor is a closed hand over a folder it may move into and a not-allowed sign everywhere else; the normal cursor is back after a drop or Escape.
- [x] ISC-241: Pressing a resize handle, the sidebar edge or a split handle between panes, gives the handle keyboard focus, so the Left and Right arrow keys resize it and never move the editor caret.
- [x] ISC-242: The browser's own right-click menu (the one with "Inspect") never shows anywhere in the app except on editor text and text boxes; the tab menu and the tree menu still open. (The exception for text was removed on 2026-11-11 by ISC-251.)
- [x] ISC-243: Copy Path and Copy Relative Path write the same kind of separator: backslashes for a Windows path, forward slashes for any other; the relative path never mixes the two.
- [x] ISC-244: No error shown to the user contains an OS error code; saving to a file the OS marks read-only says the file is read-only and points to Save As, and other I/O failures are worded in plain sentences.
- [x] ISC-245: A tab opened for the first time in a pane starts at its caret (the top for a new file), whatever the tab before it was scrolled to; a tab that was shown before comes back to the place it was left.
- [x] ISC-246: A file the user picks by hand (a tree click, Quick Open, the open dialog) gets a tab in the active pane even when another pane shows it, sharing one document, text and undo; links, history, references and recent files still jump to the tab that shows the file.
- [x] ISC-247: A pane's tab strip scrolls sideways with no visible scrollbar, its tabs never shrink below 96 px, the active tab is kept in view, and two buttons stay fixed at its right end: + makes a new file in that pane and a chevron lists the pane's tabs.
- [x] ISC-248: Every scrolling area (editor, sidebar tree, palette and menu lists, panels) shows a thin, rounded, themed scrollbar with no arrow buttons, in both themes; the tab strip shows none. (With more than one pane only the focused pane shows its thumbs: ISC-546.)
- [x] ISC-249: A setting (and a palette command) turns the F12 developer tools on or off while the app runs; a release build has them off until the setting turns them on.
- [x] ISC-250: A tab with unsaved changes shows its dot centred exactly where the close button's X appears, in the same 20 px slot, and the tab's label does not change width when the pointer enters the tab.
- [x] ISC-251: The browser's right-click menu (Inspect, Cut, Copy, Paste) never appears anywhere in the app, editor text and text boxes included; the tab menu and the file tree menu still open.
- [x] ISC-252: Renaming `A/a.md` to `A/b.md` rewrites only the links that lead to `A/a.md`: a link to `B/a.md` is left alone, whether it sits in a file at the root, in `A` or in `B`.
- [x] ISC-253: The scans behind rename, move and References read only Markdown files (md, markdown, mdown, mkd, mdx, any case) and never enter a `node_modules` folder (since Phase 7.1, none of the built-in excluded folders of ISC-282); Quick Open lists every file outside those folders.
