- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-258 to ISC-265 for P7-T1 (replace as one undo step, read-only files, the 10 MB file, matches inside hidden syntax, the keys, focus on close, the bar per pane, whole word). Whole word came from the owner's review of the Phase 7 report. The find panel is a Vue bar in each pane, not CodeMirror's own panel, because that panel is dropped by every `setState` and cannot hold the app's components; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-266 to ISC-273 for P7-T2 (what folder search reads, lines and columns, the same query meaning as Find, unsaved text, the virtual list and its cap, the keys and focus of the results, the Search tab and its command, stopping and failing). Folder search matches by line in Rust's `regex` with the app's own file walker instead of the `ignore` and `grep-searcher` crates the plan named; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-274 and changed the journal (made before P7-T3 because it showed up in its speed gate). It now writes one interval (1000 ms) after the last change, and at the latest 5 s (`JOURNAL_MAX_WAIT_MS`) after the first change when changes keep coming; before, it wrote one interval after the first change, so typing in a dirty 1 MB file wrote a 1 MB snapshot about once a second. Why: the 2026-11-07 decision above worked out "1 late frame in 60" and said it was not measured with the journal live. P7 measured it: in a 1 MB file the p95 work per key was 14.9 to 16.1 ms with the journal on and 7.2 to 7.5 ms with it off (`typing-outline-open-trusted`, 5 runs each), so the journal put a normal key at the edge of the 16 ms gate. With the new rule the same scenario reads 8.5 ms (7.9 to 8.7), repeatable. Cost: a force-kill during typing that never pauses for a second loses up to 5 s of it (it was 1 s); after any pause of a second nothing is lost beyond the last second, as before. Supersedes "at most a second of typing lost" in the 2026-11-07 line. Easy to change: `JOURNAL_MAX_WAIT_MS` (and `JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS`) in `src/documents/session.ts`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-275 to ISC-280 for P7-T3 (the scan rules, three scans that agree, the list and its keys, the tab and its messages, when it scans, and the typing anti-claim) and closed ISC-141 to ISC-144. Decisions: the Rust scan is a plain line scan whose result the page turns into display text only for the rows on screen; a heading list is capped at 50,000; the wait after typing is 400 ms and only a change of text restarts it. The typing gate was missed once and led to the journal change (ISC-274), see the decision line above and ISC-280.
- 2026-11-12: Chose a workspace index (Phase 7.1) over honouring `.gitignore` to make large roots fast. Quick Open walked every root each time the picker opened, and link scans and folder search walked on every request. A root-level `.gitignore` would also hide unversioned documentation folders the owner wants searchable, so the index skips a built-in list of build and dependency folder names instead, editable in Phase 8. Settled with the owner: paths and metadata only, a versioned disk cache per root with a background reconcile, updates from mdmate's own operations and a recursive watcher, one entry per file under nested roots (innermost root), a 1,000,000-file cap, and an Indexing label with a percent bar in the status bar. Replaces the `.gitignore` fog line.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-281 to ISC-294 for Phase 7.1 (the index and its exclusions, the cache and the start-up reconcile, updates from mdmate and from the watcher, nested roots, the cap, the status bar, Quick Open and its speed gate, the typing anti-claim, and the link scans and folder search on the index).
- 2026-11-12: refined: ISC-236 now says the status bar holds a message or the find match count, because the owner moved the file path to the title bar (left-aligned, replacing the app name) and the match count into the status bar; the find bar got its switches (regular expression first, larger icons) before the box, Find and Find Prev buttons after it, equal-length boxes, and a more vivid match color.
- 2026-11-12: Chose VS Code's pattern rules for folder search "files to include" and "files to exclude" (globs, not regular expressions), VS Code's Find in Folder entry points and strings, and kept `.git` and `node_modules` as hard skips until Phase 7.1 / 8. Settled with the owner. The Stop button was never seen because it exists only while a search runs and has no fill, so it becomes an outlined button with a progress bar. Task P7-T4; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-295 to ISC-303 for P7-T4 (toggles in the box, the two fields and their grammar, filters and pruning, bad patterns, Find in Folder, roots, the Stop button, and no search while typing); ISC-273 now names the Stop button.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-304 and ISC-305 after the first run of P7-T4: Ctrl+Shift+F leaves include as it was (as in VS Code), so a leftover pattern limited searches with nothing on screen saying so; the `...` button now carries a dot and the result line names the scope.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-306 after the first run of P7-T4: a result opened its file with the caret at the start of the match and nothing drawn, and with focus left in the list the editor draws no selection; the match is now marked until the caret moves.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-307 and ISC-308 (P7-T5): Close All now closes only the pane it was asked in (it used to close every tab of every pane), and the tab menu gets Close to the Right, also within its pane (owner call). ISC-211 now says so.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-309 and ISC-310 (P7-T6): the places of the Find query and of the folder search hits show as ticks along the editor scrollbar, as in Sublime Text (owner call). ISC-309 now also marks every hit in the text: after the first run only the clicked hit was drawn there, while the ticks showed them all.
- 2026-11-13: Phase 7.1 build choices, in `ai-docs/phases/phase-07.1/decisions.md`: the plan said Windows reports dropped watcher events, but the `notify` crate (8.2.1) cannot say so on Windows, so a burst of 64 or more events and every start-up reconcile stand in for it; own-operation updates sit in the Rust operations and not in `ops.ts`; the cache is written 500 ms after the last change; Quick Open follows the index while it is first built; the link scans and folder search list files from a root the index holds whole and walk any other. ISC-121 and ISC-253 now say the built-in excluded folders are not entered.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-313 and ISC-314 after the owner reviewed Ctrl+P: a search for "agents.md" listed `AgentSystem.md`, `multi-agent-support-plan.md`, `LeveragePoints.md`, `Max.md` (through a folder named agents) and others whose letters only sit in that order somewhere in the path. Settled with the owner: names only unless the query has a slash (as in Obsidian and VS Code), and the typed text must appear in the name as written; the old letters-in-order matching stays only as a fallback when no name has the text, so initials like `pln` still work (ISC-130). Phase 7.1, task P7.1-T4; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07.1/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-315 to ISC-320 (P7.1-T5) after the owner pressed Enter on a rename with `D:\develop\projects` as a workspace root and waited for the link scan before being asked whether to update links. Settled with the owner: the question comes first (no counts), "Don't update" is the first and focused button, a line says what was updated afterwards, drag-moves follow the same rule, and a setting can switch link scanning off altogether. ISC-320 is the Phase 8 half: `links.onRename` (`ask`, `update`, `never`) in `settings.json`; until then the default is `never` and `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` sets it. ISC-126 and ISC-127 say so in their own lines, and the old `links.confirmRewrite` flag is gone (`true` is `ask`, `false` is `update`). Phase 7.1, task P7.1-T5; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07.1/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-321 (P7.1-T5) after the owner asked whether a link scan reads every workspace root: it did, and should read only the root that holds the file. Settled with the owner; the same rule applies to Find File References so the list shows what a rename would update. The exclusions needed no change: ISC-282, ISC-294 and ISC-253 already keep the built-in folders and non-Markdown files out of every scan. A link from a different root into the file is no longer updated; the owner chose that. See `ai-docs/phases/phase-07.1/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-13: refined: ISC-316 now names the buttons Yes, No and Cancel, with Yes first and focused (it was Don't update first), after the owner reasoned that a user who turned link scanning on in settings wants the links updated; the note under the question is shorter. Behaviour of each answer is unchanged. Phase 7.1, task P7.1-T5.
- 2026-11-13: refined: ISC-318 report lists are folded behind a chevron and drawn one line per file, name coloured, after the owner saw them wrapping over four lines (P7.1-T5).
- 2026-11-13: refined: Phase 9 settled with the owner in a design session: it runs before Phase 8; a click selects an image and shows a width-only handle; a resize writes `<img src alt width>`, so mdmate now also draws `<img>` (new ISC-322 to ISC-330; ISC-154, 157, 158, 159 reworded). The paste folder is `assets` next to the document until P8-T1 makes it the `images.pasteFolder` setting (ISC-330). See `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-13: refined: untitled documents get a draft folder per document for pasted and copied images (new P9-T5; ISC-327 reworded, ISC-331 to ISC-333 added), chosen by the owner over base64 in the text (a bigger session snapshot, a CSP `data:` exception) and over asking to Save As on the first paste. Until P9-T5 an untitled paste refuses with a notice.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-334 (P9-T1 follow-up) after the owner pasted a picture, moved the caret and pressed undo: the text went and the picture stayed drawn, because an edit that deletes a block's whole text leaves an empty widget on the edge of the rescanned lines, and the rescan kept anything that only touched that edge. The rescan now drops empty blocks. A random-edit test then found a related older gap with tables (Remaining Work).
- 2026-11-13: refined: ISC-159 gained one stated exception, found by a parser probe in P9-T4: `<img ...>` alone on a line starts an HTML block that runs to the next blank line, so a resized Markdown image that was the first line of a paragraph with a caption or another picture on the next line would swallow it (raw HTML in every other viewer, and mdmate would stop drawing the next picture). The resize adds one line break after the new tag in exactly that case; nothing else outside the image's source changes. The alternative, leaving the next line inside the block, was rejected because it breaks neighbouring Markdown silently. The owner can reverse this by dropping the `joinsNext` rule in `blocks/imageEdit.ts`.
- 2026-11-13: found in the real window and fixed in Phase 9: after a first Save As (or a file move) the pictures whose link text did not change kept the address of the draft folder that was just removed, because the block field only rescans on an edit or a selection. A new `imagesMoved` effect, sent to every tab by `Document.relocate` and `documentMoved`, makes the field resolve every picture again. Test: `tests/editor/images.test.ts` (`a folder that changes under unchanged text`); real window: after Save As all three pictures read `ws2/assets/...` and loaded.
- 2026-11-13: the block field now remembers where hidden blocks are and rescans a whole top-level block, fixing the table-extents gap in Remaining Work (owner decision: fix it in Phase 9). The owner also accepted the reworded ISC-333 (the scope is app-wide; what separates tabs is the page allowlist) and the ISC-159 `joinsNext` exception.
- 2026-11-13: P9-T5 build choices, in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`: Rust owns the draft folders (a number from the page keys `drafts/<n>`; every deletion path is built from a checked number, skips links and junctions, and a failed removal is left for the start-up sweep); the sweep runs only after a restore with no notice, so a damaged session never costs a draft; the first Save As copies only the images the text links to and renames the links in Rust before writing, with the same rule in the page; ISC-327, 332 and 333 stay open for the real window.
- 2026-11-13: P9-T4 build choices, in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`: the click sets a per-view flag and leaves the caret alone; `<img>` edits are attribute-level; a Markdown image is rewritten whole, or not at all when the tag would not read back the same.
- 2026-11-13: P9-T3 build choices, in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`: the tag is read by a small allowlist parser (`blocks/imageTag.ts`) that keeps five attributes and refuses anything that is not exactly one `<img>` on one line; an `<img>` inside other inline HTML is drawn and that HTML stays source; hostile.md gained six hostile tags through its generator.
- 2026-11-13: P9-T2 build choices, in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`: Rust decides link-in-place or copy from its own drop record and the real (canonical) path, using the same innermost workspace folder the page uses as the allowlist boundary; the link goes in at the drop point; ISC-156 and ISC-328 stay open for the real OLE drop at the end of the phase.
- 2026-11-14: refined: one picture is one file, and a dropped file keeps its name (owner, after trying Phase 9: "if I drop the same image 10 times, that could mean the same image is added 10 times with different names"). New ISC-335 to ISC-337; ISC-154 and ISC-329 reworded. A picture whose bytes are already in the destination images folder is linked there, not written again (paste, drop and the Save As copy of draft images); the copy of a dropped file keeps its own name, cleaned (see ISC-336), where a paste keeps the stamp name. Reversed from the P9-T1 choice "the file keeps the stamp name": that choice avoided cleaning names, and the owner preferred readable names plus no duplicates. See `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-13: refined: ISC-154 now says a copied JPEG, GIF or WebP keeps the extension of its own type (a screenshot is `.png`). The P9-T1 check in the real window showed that an Explorer file copy arrives as a `File` with its real name, type and bytes in `clipboardData.files`, like a screenshot, so one path handles both; saving JPEG bytes as `.png` would give a file that lies about its type. The check and the other P9-T1 choices are in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: Phase 8 gains a settings screen beside the files (new ISC-343 to ISC-385), after the owner asked for logically grouped settings and a consistent UI that edit the same JSON as hand editing. Settled in a design session: one singleton Settings tab (Ctrl+, , menu, palette, gear); controls apply instantly and write only the key they touch, a default value removes the key; `settings.json`, `keybindings.json` and `themes/` live in the app data folder and are never read from a workspace; an invalid file keeps the last good values with a banner; a dirty `settings.json` tab gets a Save/Discard/Cancel prompt before the screen writes; categories are Appearance, Editor, Files & Workspace, Keyboard, Advanced; every row shows label, description, JSON key, modified dot and reset. New settings beyond the three planned: three font families, `fonts.size`, `fonts.codeSize` and a per-level `fonts.headingScale` (defaults equal today's look), `editor.wordWrap`, `files.largeFileMB`, `recent.files`/`recent.folders`, `workspace.excludedFolders`. Themes grow a Sepia built-in (MarkdownWeave's palette, darkened where it fails the contrast floors, syntax colours designed here) and System, plus a Customize copy and an on-screen editor for all 54 tokens; keybindings get an on-screen recorder with conflict Replace. `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` stays as a launch-time override and locks its row. Departs from the P8-T1 plan, which was files only.
- 2026-11-14: refined: ISC-367 said "the 20 built-in excluded folders"; `EXCLUDED_DIRS` in `fs/exclude.rs` holds 21 (counted in P8-T1), and the plan's "20 names" came from the same miscount. The claim now names no count (it says 21 today) and its probe compares the page's list with the Rust file, so a third copy of the number cannot rot. Not a softening: the claim still asks for the whole built-in list.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1 builds the settings core. One validator, in the page (`src/lib/settingsFile.ts`, a table of every key with its default and check); Rust keeps only the file (read, atomic write, watch, grant the one file to open as a tab) and takes no path from the page, which is what ISC-384 rests on. A key with a bad value keeps its last good value (the first load, before any good value, uses the default); a key the file lacks goes back to its default. What `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` does once the file exists (ISC-320): while it holds ask, update or never it wins over the file for that run; with it unset the file decides. New ISC-386 (a failed write or a file that is not JSON changes nothing and says why). ISC-349 to ISC-351 are checked at the store boundary in P8-T1 (`changeSetting`/`resetSetting`, the file watcher), in the file the plan names; the mounted rows and controls add their cases to the same file in P8-T1d, which is where ISC-355 and ISC-356 close. ISC-368 names the row that shows the refusal, which does not exist until P8-T1d, so, as with ISC-349 to ISC-353, it stays open and moves to P8-T1d in `ai-docs/tasks.md`; P8-T1b proved the refusal in Rust and the page, the last good folder staying in use, and the page’s problems record, recorded as an evidence note in Verification (progress 327/386). ISC-249 names a palette command that turns the setting on and off (P8-T1f) and a release build (waits for the owner’s permission), so it stays open too. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: ISC-349, ISC-350, ISC-351, ISC-352 and ISC-353 name a control, a row, a banner or the open Settings screen, which do not exist until P8-T1d, so they stay open and move from P8-T1 to P8-T1d (the banner text and the row flag are the T1d part) in `ai-docs/tasks.md`. P8-T1 proved the same writes and the same live update at the store boundary (`changeSetting`, in tests and in the real window), recorded as an evidence note in Verification; that is a weaker probe than the claim, so it is not closed on it. progress is 322/386. P8-T1 also closes ISC-386, which it added.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1b. (1) A paste-folder part allows only letters, digits, `-`, `_` and `.` (no spaces, no `%`, `#`, `?`, `(`, `)`), stricter than the plan's "relative, no `..`, no drive": a space or a bracket in the folder would make the pasted link not plain Markdown, and a page validator and Rust that disagree is the failure ISC-368 guards against, so both read the 46 cases in `tests/data/paste-folders.json`. (2) A draft keeps its fixed `assets` folder (nothing is written next to a file that does not exist); `images.pasteFolder` applies at Save As time, which copies the used images into it and rewrites the draft's links to it, also for an image whose name did not change. ISC-330 reads "the next paste" for a file with a folder and "the next Save As" for a draft. (3) The page pushes validated runtime values to Rust with one command, `settings_apply`, which checks them again and refuses the whole batch if one is wrong (a value that guards a boundary is not taken on the page's word). (4) Developer tools: `tauri` gets the `devtools` feature, so `open_devtools` exists in a release build; the window config turns the native F12 off (`"devtools": false`) and the command `devtools_open` opens them only while the setting is on. WebView2 cannot close an open developer-tools window from the API on Windows, so turning the setting off stops new opens and leaves an open window open. ISC-249 stays open: its release-build half needs the owner's permission to build one. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1c. The Settings tab is a third tab kind, `settings`, whose record holds the group it shows; there is one because `openSettingsTab` looks for an existing one first (in any pane) and splitting never copies it, not because of a fixed id. A saved session that names two keeps the first in the layout's order (new ISC-388), and a tab of a kind this version does not know is left out instead of being opened as a file by its path (new ISC-387, which also covers a session written by a later version). A saved group name this version does not know falls back to the first group with no notice. The group is not in the layout, so a change to it bumps `tabRecordsVersion`, which the session journal watches; without that a group chosen by a click would reach `session.json` only with the next layout change. The gear is in the title bar, between the file name and the window buttons (the plan says only "a gear button" and names no place; say so if it belongs elsewhere). Until P8-T1d the screen shows the five groups and "This group has no settings yet." under each. The plan says this task's view loads lazily "like the palette and the search panel"; no claim covered the architecture rule for that yet, so the `settings-lazy` rule and its test are new ISC-389. Open settings.json opens the file as an ordinary text tab; the prompt for unsaved edits in it is P8-T1d's. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1d. Every row is one `SettingRow` (label, description, control slot, key, dot and reset while modified, error and lock notes), drawn from the table in `features/settings/rows.ts`, which holds only wording and grouping: values, defaults and checking stay in `SETTING_DEFS`. A control never holds a value. A switch or choice asks the store for the change at once; a number or text field keeps what is typed as a draft and checks it with the same `check` when it is left or Enter is pressed, and a refused value shows its reason on the row and writes nothing. The Appearance group has only Theme (one choice, Dark, until the themes of P8-T3), the fonts are P8-T1e's rows, and Keyboard has none until P8-T2, so it says so. ISC-358 stays open: only Dark exists to look at, and the plan moves it to P8-T3 with the other themes (tasks.md moves it in the P8-T1f commit). The unsaved-edits question (ISC-354) lives in the settings store, so a change is held in `pendingSettingChange` and the screen only shows the question: Save writes the tab and then makes the change, Don't save reloads the tab from the file (new `revertDocument`, which `resolvePendingRevert` now uses too) and makes it, Cancel drops it; a failed save keeps the question open. A change that is written now also brings an open, clean `settings.json` tab up to date through `refreshFromDisk`, without waiting for the file watcher (new ISC-391; the browser mock's document watcher never reports the page's own settings writes, and the real watcher was not relied on for this). The generated Switch styled itself with `data-checked:` and `data-unchecked:`, which Reka does not set, so it was invisible; tokens.css now defines those two variants (new ISC-390). `checkFolderName` accepts `..` as an excluded-folder name; it can never match a directory entry, so it is harmless and left as it is. The Links row, when `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` is set, shows the variable's value, is disabled and drops its reset icon. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1e. The document fonts reach the editor as CSS custom properties on the page's root (`--doc-font-body`, `--doc-font-heading`, `--doc-font-code`, `--doc-size`, `--doc-code-ratio`, `--doc-heading-1` to `-6`), set by `editor/documentFonts.ts` from the live settings and read only by the editor's theme, so nothing outside the document can pick them up (new ISC-392). A family is written as the owner likes and is turned into a list of quoted names ending in the built-in stack, because an unquoted name that starts with a digit is invalid CSS and would drop the whole declaration instead of falling back. Headings fall back to the body family. The new look at the defaults is not the old look: the editor drew 13 px text (it inherited the window's), and the plan's default `fonts.size` is 15 with code at 14 (it was 0.92 of 13, about 12 px); the heading multiples are unchanged, so headings grow with the body. The code size is held as a multiple of the text size (`codeSize / size`) and code is drawn at `calc(ratio * 1em)`: code in body text and in a block is exactly `fonts.codeSize`, and inline code in a heading grows with the heading, as it did when it was `0.92em` (a first version held px and drew code in an H1 smaller than the heading). Code in a table sits in text drawn at 0.95em, so it is 0.95 x `fonts.codeSize`. Each family field also shows a sample line in the family typed, before it is accepted (new ISC-393).
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T1f. A `Command` gains an optional `state()` (true is On) that the palette rows and the menu items show as On or Off, read from the live setting like `enabled`. Toggle Word Wrap, Toggle Developer Tools and Switch Theme are commands; the two toggles go through `requestSettingChange`, the same path as the Settings screen, so unsaved edits in the settings.json tab hold the change and the Settings tab is shown, where the question is (new ISC-394), and nothing is written until it is answered. Switch Theme is a third palette mode, `themes`, a pick list of `THEME_CHOICES` (moved to `lib/themes.ts` so the palette does not pull in the screen's row table) with the theme in use marked. Only Dark exists, so what is proven now is the list, the mark and the write path (new ISC-395); ISC-371 (Dark, Light, Sepia, System and every user theme file) and ISC-358 (the screen in three themes) need the themes and move to P8-T3. ISC-249 stays open: the command half is done, the release-build half waits for the owner's permission to build one.
- 2026-11-14: refined: a tooltip has its own surface, `--tooltip` and `--tooltip-foreground` (owner: it must stand out from the app background). Dark is lighter than the page; Light stays the popover color, since a darker tooltip did not suit it (owner, same day). The shadcn `TooltipContent` and the in-editor `.editor-hint` use them, so the theme has 56 tokens, not 54: ISC-381 and the P8-T3 plan now say 56.
- 2026-11-14: refined: P8-T2 design. (1) `keybindings.json` is one flat object from command id to shortcut text, or to `null` for no shortcut; ids contain dots (`view.toggleSidebar`) and stay one key, never a path. A row's reset removes its key; Reset all writes `{}` (the plan says "clears the file"), which also drops keys for ids this build does not know, while a change of one row keeps them like ISC-349. (2) Overrides are resolved against the final set of bindings, not pairwise against the defaults, and the key order in the file means nothing: a swap works; when two commands' resulting shortcuts are the same, every override involved is dropped (the command goes back to its default) and the notice names the commands and the shortcut, repeated until nothing collides, since a default coming back can collide in turn. A default never loses to an override. Registering two built-in defaults on one shortcut still throws: that is a programmer error, bad user data never throws. (3) The ISC-374 row named `Mod+B` and Toggle Sidebar, but `Mod+B` is Bold and Toggle Sidebar is `Mod+Shift+B`; the row now records `Mod+Shift+B` on Bold. (4) New ISC-402 to ISC-411 for what the plan left implicit: a rebind beats CodeMirror's own keys in an open editor, `null`, the dirty `keybindings.json` tab, the recorder's edges, `bindingFromEvent`, Reset all, Open keybindings.json (the plan put it in Advanced, T1d did not add it), the banner, and a live label everywhere. (5) Rust gets a second file on the same code (`KeybindingsStore`, fixed name, no path from the page), so ISC-384 covers it. (6) ISC-411 first named the tab strip too; it was narrowed to the Keyboard row, the palette and the menu, the ones the test reads, since the tab strip and the tree menu call the same reactive function but were not mounted.
- 2026-11-14: refined: Keyboard page groups and search (owner-requested, decided in a grill with all answers agreed). (1) Six groups in a fixed order, each command naming exactly one: File 10, Find 5, Formatting 7, View 7, Tabs & Navigation 6, App 6; the `group` field is required on `Command` and `registerCommand` refuses an unlisted one, so a new command cannot be left off the page. Group names show only on the Keyboard page, not in the palette. (2) One scrolling page with a heading per group, rows A to Z inside, no collapsing; a group with no match has no heading. (3) The search box sits beside the "Keyboard" heading (not inside the `<h1>`, so the heading and the section label stay "Keyboard"); it uses the Search settings rule (every word must be part of the row) over title, id, group name and the shortcut as shown now and by default, with the refinement that a word holding a plus must equal whole plus-separated parts of one shortcut. (4) Search settings still lists no shortcut. (5) Starting to use the box cancels a recording through the shortcut button's blur (the capture listener keeps every key from the box while a row records), and the box starts empty each time a category is chosen. New ISC-412 to ISC-419.
- 2026-11-14: refined: settings.json and keybindings.json are JSON with comments (owner chose it after rejecting a read-only defaults tab; the full reasons are in `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`). Both read comments and trailing commas; opening either while it holds nothing writes a commented template of every setting or command; an edit changes only its key and never a comment; an entry taken out gets its commented example back, so all-defaults reads like the first template; Reset all does the same. Deviation from "template on open only": the first change from the screen to a file that holds nothing also starts from the template (ISC-429). The docs that said "strict JSON" (`architecture.md` and the phase `plan.md`) were reworded. New ISC-420 to ISC-430.
- 2026-11-14: refined: the restore (decision above) only runs in a file that carries the template, found by its first header comment. Owner screenshot: a `settings.json` that held an entry from before the template existed, after that entry was reset, held a lone commented `fonts` block that looked like a second `fonts` object. A file written by hand, or with its header deleted, now just loses the entry and gains no comment; a file left holding nothing takes the whole template on the next change (ISC-429). In a full template, a live group still sits above the commented block of the same group, which lists the other keys. New ISC-431.

(2) Reset all moved from the Keyboard page to Advanced, one per file, each in the row of its file and behind a confirmation dialog (Cancel focused; Escape and Cancel change nothing). The visible label is "Reset all" and the accessible name is "Reset all settings" or "Reset all shortcuts": the full label made the two buttons wrap into a stack in the 16rem control column, and the owner asked for them beside each other. The dialog and the aria-label name the file. A reset asked for while the file's tab holds unsaved edits shows the existing save-first question, now one shared bar (SaveFirstQuestion) on the Settings screen for both files, because the keybindings question used to live only on the Keyboard page. (3) A --warning token, since none existed: Dark oklch(0.82 0.14 82), Light oklch(0.55 0.12 68). A first Light value (0.5 0.09 72) passed the tests but looked brown and close to the grey text in the window, so it was lightened and made more saturated; both pass the 4.5 contrast and sRGB tests. - 2026-11-15: owner request, the two settings `editor.limitContentWidth` and `editor.contentMaxWidth` took effect nowhere. They now limit the text column of editor tabs. The limit is one custom property on the page's root (`--doc-content-max`, `calc(width + 2 * 1.5rem)` because the setting counts the text and not the padding, or `none`), read only by the editor theme as `max-width` with `margin-inline: auto`; the theme's own fallback is the default, so the first paint is already right. A change re-sets the property and asks each open view to measure (`documentWidth.ts`). Reading of "not the Settings / Keybindings tabs": the Settings screen is a Vue view with no editor, so it is exempt by construction; the `settings.json` and `keybindings.json` editor tabs keep the whole width, found by file name (any folder, any case, and following a rename). Flip: drop `fullWidthFor` in `document.ts` to limit them too. While word wrap is off the limit steps aside (`.cm-content:not(.cm-lineWrapping)`): CodeMirror gives a non-wrapping content box the width of its longest line, which beats `max-width`, so a limit there would be drawn wrong or cut a line. The definitions of the two settings (`settings.ts`, `settingsFile.ts`, `rows.ts`) were uncommitted work from another session; this commit carries them. ISC-439 to ISC-443.

- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T3 design. (1) The palette is the root's `data-theme` (`dark`, `light`, `sepia`) and darkness is the root's `dark` class, set only when the palette's `color-scheme` is dark, so Tailwind's `dark:` variants follow darkness and not a name: Sepia and any user theme based on a light palette never switch them on (`@custom-variant dark` stays as it was; `index.html` starts with `class="dark"` and `data-theme="dark"`, and the Tauri window keeps Dark's background, pinned by `tests/styles/themes.test.ts`). (2) There are 57 tokens, counted from the CSS (the plan said 56); the editor's groups are Core 21, Sidebar 5, Chrome 13, Editor 4 and Syntax 14, and `lib/themeTokens.ts` holds the list and the 31 contrast pairs once for the test and the editor. (3) A user theme is `themes/<name>.json`, JSON with comments: `{ "name"?, "base": dark|light|sepia, "colors": { token: value } }`, token names without the dashes. A value passes only if it is `#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa` or `oklch()` with plain numbers; not `var()`, `url()`, `rgb()` or a named color, so what reaches the styles has the shape the contrast code reads. A bad value is dropped (the token keeps its base color) with a notice; a file that does not parse, or a selected name with no file, shows Dark with a notice and leaves `settings.json` as written; a file named like a built-in (any case, and `system`) is ignored with one notice. Applying a theme goes through one function that checks each value again before `style.setProperty`. (4) The built-in values are read from the CSS files with `?raw` and the same declaration parser, so the CSS stays the one source and Customize copies what the browser has; vitest returns an empty string for `?raw` CSS unless `css.include` names the theme files (found when the Sepia tests saw 0 tokens), so `vite.config.ts` names them. (5) Rust: `ThemeStore` (list, read, write with a `create` flag, watch, ensure folder), no path from the page, a plain name plus the fixed `.json`, 256 KB per file, 200 files, only plain files (a link or folder is skipped and refused), a create never replaces. The name rules are tested against one data file, `tests/data/theme-names.json`, by both sides. The settings watcher loop became generic (`run` takes the event filter) instead of being copied. (6) Customize writes literal colors for every token of what is shown (System copies the Dark or Light the OS has made current; `var()` aliases are resolved), named `<Theme> copy`, then `copy 2` and so on, and takes the next name if a file appeared in the meantime; it then selects the copy through the settings screen's own path, so the unsaved-edits question still applies. (7) The colour editor shows every token of the theme in force, in groups, only for a user theme; a built-in shows a Customize button. A text field per token checks what is typed (Enter or leaving the field accepts, Escape drops); the picker exists only for a color with no alpha, because `<input type=color>` would drop it. A change is shown on the page at once and written 150 ms after the last one, read fresh from the file first and edited with `jsonc-parser` so comments and unknown keys stay; writes run one after another; a file that is not valid JSON is refused and left alone. A write that lands while a newer color is still pending re-shows the pending one (a test found the older file color flashing back). The contrast warnings are advisory and use the base's color for a token the file leaves out. (8) Open themes folder is a button on Advanced only. The plan and ISC-457 also named a palette command, but the owner agreed the exact 41 commands and their groups (ISC-412, `tests/workbench/defaultCommands.test.ts`), so the command was not added and ISC-457 now says so. (9) Sepia ports MarkdownWeave's cream page `#fdf6e3`, brown text `#3b2f1e`, links `#8b5e3c`; its grey notes `#8a7060` (4.26:1 on the page) and `#b8a090` (2.30:1) fall under the floors, so the greys are darker, and the syntax colors, `--warning` and the rest are designed for the page. Its own test then found three literal values copied from Light (`--find-match`, `--find-match-active`, `--syntax-number`); each now has its own. ISC-146 (no SublimeText assets) stays open for the owner: Dark and Light are the app's own and Sepia comes from the owner's MarkdownWeave. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.

- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T4 design. (1) The data folder is chosen in this order: `MDMATE_DATA_DIR` when set and not empty, then `<exe dir>/data` when a file named `mdmate.portable` is beside the exe, then the platform's app-data folder, then a temp folder. The environment variable wins over the marker because it is the one explicit, per-launch choice: tests and the perf harness set it to a fresh folder so they never touch a real session, and a copy of the app carrying a marker must not defeat that. (2) The marker has to be a file (`fs::metadata(..).is_file()`): a folder called `mdmate.portable` is not one, and only the exe's own folder is looked at, never the working folder or a parent. (3) Riftle's `paths.rs` was the model for the marker idea but not for its failures: it `expect`s on a missing exe path and `create_dir_all`s with a panic. Here an unreadable exe path or folder means "not portable" and the usual folder is used (ISC-461); no folder is created by the lookup, each store makes its own on first write, as before. (4) `data_dir` takes the exe folder as a parameter, so the tests inject a scratch folder and never depend on where the test binary sits. (5) The WebView2 profile stays where Tauri puts it (LocalAppData), so portable mode moves the app's own data and not the browser engine's cache. This is my reading of "moves all data there" and the owner confirmed on 2026-11-15 to leave it as is: the profile holds engine caches, not user data, and moving it needs a startup environment setting before the window exists; the README (P10) still has to say so. (6) `cfg(windows)` is not needed: the lookup uses only `std::env::current_exe` and `std::fs`, so the arch rule on OS code outside `platform/` is untouched. Details: `ai-docs/phases/phase-08/decisions.md`.

- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T5 design (owner request after trying P8-T3). (1) Every sub-group heading on the Settings screen is one component, `SubGroupHeading.vue`, drawn in `--primary`: Theme, Fonts and Theme colors on Appearance, the five color groups, and the Keyboard groups. This reverses the 2026-11-14 request for headings in the text color; the owner asked for it, and `primary on background` is already a contrast pair at 4.5. The page title stays in the text color. A row names its sub-group in `SETTING_ROWS` (`group`), and the screen draws a heading where the group changes, none in a search. (2) `themes.hideContrastWarnings` is true by default: the owner first said "off by default", then, shown the switch's name, "Hide contrast warnings" on by default, so no contrast line shows until it is turned off. A color under its floor is written either way. (3) `themes.colorFormat` is `hex` or `oklch` and changes only how colors are shown and written. A field shows a color as written when it is already in the chosen format and converts it otherwise; typing either format is accepted and is written in the chosen one; the picker writes the chosen one. A value is converted only when it is edited, so a file is never rewritten by changing the format (ISC-470). (4) The OKLab conversion is Ottosson's, in `lib/color.ts`, printed with 5 decimals for lightness and chroma and 3 for hue so that hex to oklch to hex is exact on a grid of 4,913 colors; with 4 decimals one color (#00d0d0) came back one step off. The function name is kept in a constant because the arch scan forbids a color literal outside `src/styles`. (5) Found by the first test of the setting: `assignSettings` copies each settings group by hand, so a new group has to be added there or it never loads from `settings.json`.
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 design (owner request, grill rounds 1 and 2). (1) A theme has a free display name (the `name` in its file, 1 to 60 printable characters, unique among themes ignoring case, never a built-in name); the file name is derived from it (`themeStem`: characters a file cannot have become `-`, a taken or built-in file name gets a number on the file only) and is the theme's identity, so Duplicate of "Mine" is now "Mine copy" (it was "mine copy"). (2) One list of every theme under a "Themes" heading (built-ins first, then the user's by name); New theme is an inline form, not a modal, because the project has no Dialog component, only alert-dialog, which is kept for the delete question. Customize is now "duplicate the theme in use". (3) "Based on" is a Dark, Light, Sepia button group, not a two-way switch, since a theme can sit on Sepia; changing it first writes the colors the file leaves out from the old base, so nothing on screen changes. (4) Delete goes to the Recycle Bin through the `trash` crate; with no bin it is refused and the file stays (never a permanent delete); a theme in use gives way to Dark with a notice. Rename and delete hold the "theme not found" notice (`holdThemeNotices`) while the file system catches up, otherwise renaming the theme in use would toast an error. (5) A reset button shows only on a color that differs from the base palette, compared as colors, so a value in another format does not count as changed. (6) The editor is scrolled into view by a request counter that SettingsView watches; it waits one turn after the next tick because a New theme form that closes right after the request moves everything below it (found in the real window: the heading landed 206 px above the top; ISC-481 now checks the form is closed at scroll time). (7) The Hide contrast warnings row is not in search (`inEditor`), as the owner asked it be seen only while editing. (8) Built-in themes have a real guard, `isBuiltInTheme` in `customize.ts`: rename is refused with a reason, delete returns false and Edit does nothing; before it, a built-in id was only refused by accident (Rust said `not-found` and a toast followed). (9) Deviations from the plan, recorded: no per-row "Show in folder" (the host has only "open the themes folder", which the Appearance page already offers as the "Open themes folder" button at its end, so a row action would do the same thing); no dot beside the per-color reset icon (the icon itself marks a changed color).
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 follow-up (owner, after seeing the list). (1) A theme row ends with one button of one width (5rem): Select, or for the theme in use a disabled "In use" in the theme's own `success` color (text, outline and a 10% background); everything else (Edit colors, Rename, Duplicate, Delete) is in an ellipsis menu after it whose trigger has no border and no background, even under the pointer. A built-in's menu holds Duplicate only. (2) `success` is a new theme token (the 58th, in Core, next to `warning`, with a 4.5 contrast pair against the background): the arch rule forbids Tailwind palette colors outside src/styles, and a theme token is how the project adds a color; a theme file that omits it takes it from its base, so no existing theme changes. (3) Each swatch has a tooltip naming its token and what it is for (Background, Card, Foreground, Primary, Accent). (4) Duplicate (row menu) and Customize or Duplicate above the colors open one form, the one New theme uses, with the name "<name> copy" selected and no palette choice, and the screen scrolls it to the middle; Customize no longer writes anything itself. `copyTheme(of, name)` checks the name like New theme does. (5) Found in the real window: an open menu traps the focus, so a field that asks for focus at once is refused and the menu then gives the focus back to its button. The menu's close-auto-focus is prevented only for Rename and Duplicate, and their fields take the focus one turn after the menu has closed; any other item leaves the focus on the ellipsis button, where a keyboard user was. (6a) The outline button brings its own dark-scheme grey border and background (dark:bg-input, dark:border-input) that a plain bg-success/10 does not replace, so the In use button sets both dark: classes too, and a test checks the merged class list for the grey. (6) On the Advanced page the three file rows end in a right-aligned flex line and the three buttons are 10.25rem wide, so their right edges meet.
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 second follow-up (owner, from screenshots). (1) "Based on" and "Start from" are one shadcn `ToggleGroup` each (added with the CLI, `@ts-nocheck` on the generated files like the others), wrapped in `ChoiceGroup.vue`: the chosen button has the primary background and text, pressing it again keeps it (reka reports "nothing chosen" then; the wrapper ignores it, so there is always an answer). The hover classes of the on state are set too, or the hover grey replaced the primary. (2) The form says "Name" with its field on the same line, for New theme and for a copy; the line about what a copy is stays under it. (3) A color with alpha has a picker: `<input type="color">` holds only `#rrggbb`, so the opacity is a separate percent field beside the swatch, and each keeps what the other set (`withOpacity` in `color.ts`). The field shows for a token that some built-in theme makes translucent (scrollbar thumb, its hover, drop preview, and find match and its active one, which the owner had not listed), or whose value has alpha now, so an opaque value can get its transparency back. A true alpha picker would need a custom widget; two controls that already exist were enough. (4) REVERSES the T6 decision "copies are verbatim": New theme, Duplicate and a base switch now write every color in the format chosen in Settings, converted when the source was in the other, because a file that mixes oklch and hex under the hex setting looked like a bug to the owner. The cost is rounding: a hex color from oklch is within one 8-bit step, and an oklch color outside sRGB is clamped.
- 2026-11-15: refined: P8-T6 third follow-up (owner: "build the real alpha picker"). (1) Every color's swatch is the trigger of one popover (`ColorPicker.vue`: shadcn Popover, added with `pnpm ui:add`); the native `<input type="color">` and the row's percent field are gone. The percent field had been there for five tokens only, and the owner found that confusing: the only way to get it elsewhere was to type two more characters into the hex. (2) The area is reka-ui's `ColorArea` (pointer, keyboard, thumb); the hue and alpha sliders are reka's `Slider` with gradient tracks, because reka's own `ColorSlider` loses the hue at grey and black (read from its source and confirmed by calling it: the hue of a grey is 0 and setting a hue on a black or grey returns hue 0). The picker holds hue, saturation, brightness and alpha itself, so a hue picked while the color is grey or black is still there when it is not; `toHsv` and `fromHsv` in `color.ts` convert with no 8-bit step lost. (3) What the picker sent is remembered (as `#rrggbbaa`), and when it comes back as the value it is not read again: reading a rounded 8-bit color back would move the hue under the pointer. A typed value, Reset or a base switch comes back as a different color and is read. (4) Open and close sends nothing: HSV is sRGB, so re-sending an oklch or out-of-gamut value would convert it. (5) The gradients and the checkerboard live in `src/styles/colorpicker.css` (the arch rule keeps color literals there), imported by the component so it ships with the lazy Settings chunk; the swatch shows a checkerboard behind a translucent color. (6) A slip of mine in the last round, fixed here: I had added toggle-group with `shadcn-vue@latest` directly. The project has `pnpm ui:add` (pinned to 2.9.0, restores protected files, marks the generated files); the toggle files were regenerated through it and are identical in content. (7) Not done: `contrast()` still treats every color as opaque, so alpha on a text or surface color can make a pair look fine when it is not.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 fourth follow-up (owner: "are all the tokens used? I changed --selection and --caret and see nothing"). A scan of the 58 tokens found four nothing read: `--selection`, `--caret`, `--handle` and `--card-foreground`. (1) The editor never drew its own selection or caret, so the browser did, in its own colors, and `create.ts` set the native caret to `--foreground`. CodeMirror's `drawSelection()` is now on in both bases (`common` and `followerBase`: a tab of a document is a follower), and the `frame` theme colors the drawn parts: `.cm-cursor` and `.cm-dropCursor` get `border-left-color: var(--caret)`; the selection is `var(--selection)` for the focused rule (as specific as CodeMirror's own), the plain one, and `.cm-content ::selection` for text inside a widget. The native `caretColor` line is gone: drawSelection hides the native caret with `!important`, so it would have been a use that does nothing, the same bug again. A side effect that is wanted: `&.cm-image-selected .cm-cursorLayer { visibility: hidden }`, which was written for this and had nothing to hide, now works. (2) `--handle` is the split handle at rest (`bg-handle` from `SplitNodeView` over the generated `ResizableHandle` by tailwind-merge; the generated file is untouched) and, at the owner's request, the image resize handle at rest, with `--handle-active` under the pointer and while dragged (`:hover`, `:active`). (3) `--card-foreground` is the text color of the New theme form, which already used `--card` for its background; its label no longer sets its own color. (4) `tests/styles/tokenUse.test.ts` fails when a token has no use (comments, the `@theme` rename lines, the theme files and the perf lab do not count). It cannot see a use that is overridden, as the caret one was, so `tests/editor/tokenColors.test.ts` checks the injected rules, and the split handle's classes have their own test because the image handle also reads `--handle` and would hide them from the scan. (5) The sidebar edge is also a resize handle, and still uses `--ring` at 40 percent; not asked for, left alone. The text drop caret (`.cm-drop-caret`) still uses `--primary`. (6) The built-in `--handle` values are the quiet splitter line colors, so the image handle at rest is nearly invisible in Dark, Light and Sepia (read from screenshots); changing the built-in values or adding an outline is the owner's call.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 fifth follow-up (owner: "--handle and --handle-active do not affect the sidebar handle, only the pane handle"). I had left the sidebar edge on `--ring` because it was not asked for; it is a resize handle like the others, so it now uses the same tokens: `bg-handle` at rest, `bg-handle-active` hovered, focused (`focus-visible`) or pressed. It is 4 px wide (the pane handle is a 1 px line with a wider hit area), so the resting color is a 4 px band, which is subtle with the built-in `--handle` and clear with a bright one. It stays in the active color after a drag while it has focus, as the old `focus-visible:bg-ring/60` did: the edge takes focus when pressed so the arrow keys resize it (ISC-241).
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 sixth follow-up (owner: selected inline code, with its background, "looks unselected"). The inline code span paints its own background above the selection, which CodeMirror draws behind the text, so a selection over code was hidden. CSS cannot tell a span is selected, so `scan.ts` cuts the span at the selection: the part a non-empty selection covers is marked `cm-md-code cm-md-code-selected` (font as code, no background, `--foreground` text) and the rest keeps `cm-md-code`. The cut is exact, so a selection that ends inside a span leaves the rest of it as code. A caret, or a selection that only touches the span's edge, changes nothing. The app has one selection range (ISC-96), so the joining of several ranges in `selectedParts` is a pure function with its own test and not a reachable case. Not covered: code inside a table cell, which is a widget with its own styles (`.cm-md-table code`); the selection there is the native one and the cell's code background still hides it.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 seventh follow-up (owner: "--handle-active applies to both the active and non-active state of the resize handle, and to the image border; the resize handle is the small square and the border round the image"). This replaces the image half of the fourth follow-up, where the square was `--handle` at rest and `--handle-active` on hover. A selected image is one state, so its outline (`.cm-md-image-selected > .cm-md-image-frame`, which was `--primary`) and its resize square are both `--handle-active` and nothing else: no rest color, no hover or pressed rule. `--handle` stays the color of the split handle and the sidebar edge at rest. ISC-502 now covers the split handle only; the image part is ISC-509.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T7 part 1 (owner: "let's build T7 from phase 08"; split with the advisor into two commits, this one the tab, the sample note and the miniature, the next the gallery and the states strip). (1) The preview is a second "panel" tab beside Settings (`isPanelTab`: no document, no caret, no host call); it is a singleton, never copied by a split, closes with no question and is left out of the saved layout (the layout is built with it closed out of a copy, so a pane that held only the preview leaves no empty pane). (2) It opens in the pane next to the Settings pane (after it, else before it, else a new pane to its right), whichever pane is focused; a second press selects it. (3) It loads with `import()` when its tab is first shown, and the arch rule `theme-preview-lazy` forbids a static import from outside `features/themes/preview/`. (4) The sample is the app's own editor on a built-in Tidewatch README, read-only and not focusable, with three selection ranges, a find query and a link status of its own, so a selection, a find match, the caret and a broken link show with no folder open; the find count event is stopped inside the tab. (5) The miniature is plain markup with the app's token classes (no live stores, no drag region); only `TabStripItem` is reused. (6) Found in the real window: a read-only CodeMirror view still moves its selection on a click and on keys, so a click in the note collapsed the three ranges into one caret. The editor now answers mousedown, touchstart, dragstart and keydown at the highest precedence and goes no further; a test clicks, double-clicks, triple-clicks and keys it and checks the ranges. (7) Also from the real window: in a 430 px pane the sidebar squeezed the note to a few words a line, so the page is a container and the miniature drops its sidebar below 32 rem, with a line saying so. (8) CodeMirror's `search()` starts with the main selection's text as its query, so the explicit query dispatch in the preview editor is redundant today; it stays so the intent does not depend on that default.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T7 part 2 (owner: "continue with the gallery and states strip please"; the second commit of the split agreed with the advisor). (1) The gallery sits under the miniature in two columns once the pane is 42 rem wide, one column below (container query, like the sidebar of the miniature). Live, real components with local state: buttons (six variants, sizes, icon, disabled), a text field, switches, the dropdown (the real Select with its real popup), a button with the real tooltip, a scrollbox. Pictures, `inert`: a tooltip, a dialog, a context menu, two toasts, a command palette. The find bar is markup with live boxes. (2) The pictures are markup with the classes copied from `components/ui/{tooltip,alert-dialog,context-menu}`, `FindBar.vue` and `PaletteHost.vue`, because the real ones live in a portal or a store and open on an action; the cost is that a class change in the app's component is a change to make in `GalleryPanels.vue` too (said in its header comment, and in the Verification entry). The toasts show the two kinds the app raises (error and warning) with the icon colors `styles/toasts.css` gives them. (3) The states strip is a fixed grid, six samples by five states. Disabled is a real `disabled`, error a real `aria-invalid`, selected a real pressed, checked or selected state; hover and focus carry the classes the control uses for them, because a pointer and a focus ring cannot be held on a dozen things at once. A cell is empty where the control has no such look (no hover on a text field, no selected switch off). The strip is `inert`: a real Tab walk showed its fourteen decorative controls as Tab stops, and a screen reader would have read a dozen controls with the same name. (4) The headings are `SubGroupHeading` (the sub-group heading of Settings, in `--primary`) and the caption of an item is a `figcaption`, so the ISC-463 scan that forbids a written-out h2 or h3 stays whole; the item prop is `label`, because the no-native-title scan reads `title=`. (5) The closed dropdown keeps an empty placeholder `div` in the page body while the tab is open and removes it with the tab; the ISC-522 test now allows exactly that and nothing else (`strangersOf`: one bare `div` with no attribute and exactly the two teleport comments; a second placeholder, an empty wrapper, a div with text, a div with two other nodes, a div with an attribute and a popup left open each count as a stray, each planted in a test) and a new test checks it goes when the tab closes. (6) Found by the full suite: the part 1 note tests read `syntaxTree(state)`, which under load can stop before the end of a long note (the table is near the end) and failed once with no table; they now finish the parse first (`wholeTree`). (7) Seen in the real window and not changed: with Settings and the preview side by side each pane is 430 px, and the Settings rows (name and control) stack into a column of one word a line; it is the Settings layout at that width, not the preview.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 eighth follow-up (owner: "the Appearance background color is controlled by --accent, but the foreground color when it has the --accent background is still using --foreground color while it should use --accent-foreground"). The Settings category buttons filled with --accent (the current one, hover, keyboard focus) set no text color of their own or set --foreground; they now set --accent-foreground wherever they set --accent, and the others stay --muted-foreground at rest. Same class of fault elsewhere, not changed (not asked): 23 `hover:bg-accent` uses in `.vue` files have no matching `hover:text-accent-foreground` (tab strip close and menu buttons, pane notices, image view buttons, title bar buttons, sidebar header button). Answer to the owner's question: `--drop-preview` is used in one place, `TabDragLayer.vue`, as the tint of the preview that shows the pane or window edge a dragged tab will land in (with a --primary border); the insertion bar in a tab strip is solid --primary.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 ninth follow-up (owner: fix the 23 hover sites, then audit the other pairs: "if --primary is used on something and it has text or an icon, that's supposed to use --primary-foreground, and so on"). One rule now: a class that fills with a color token sets that token's partner at the same state. Two token roles follow from it: `--accent` is the fill for an interactive state (hover, focus, pressed, open, current), `--muted` is a quiet static surface and its text is `--muted-foreground`. The shadcn button (outline, ghost) and toggle variants filled hover, pressed and open states with `--muted`; pairing that with `--muted-foreground` would have dimmed the text when the pointer arrives, so those states moved to `--accent` with `--accent-foreground`, like every other hover in the app. Muted banners and chips (pane notices, find bar, keyboard and save-first notices, Settings chips) now set `--muted-foreground`. Sidebar banner buttons set `--sidebar-foreground` and `--sidebar-accent-foreground`; the tree's drop-target row sets `--sidebar-accent-foreground`. The built-in themes needed no color change: every pair is already contrast-checked in `CONTRAST_PAIRS` (muted-foreground on muted and on background, accent-foreground on accent, and the rest), and accent, secondary, card, popover, tooltip and sidebar-accent text are all `var(--foreground)` in Dark, Light and Sepia, which is why the gaps only showed in a custom theme. Not done: `--destructive` has no partner token, so the error bubble in `NameInput` and the title bar close button use `text-background` on `bg-destructive`; adding `--destructive-foreground` is a theme change (tokens list, three themes, editor, contrast pair) and is the owner's call. Fills with a transparency are tints and are not enforced. The scan reads Tailwind class strings only; it does not enforce `background`, `input`, `warning`, `success` or `destructive` (no partner token for them, or the fill is a surface that text of any token legitimately sits on). The editor's CodeMirror theme objects are outside it: one gap found by hand, the markdown table header cells (`.cm-md-table th`), which fill with --muted and keep --foreground so the header stays as strong as the body; switching them to --muted-foreground would make headers dimmer than the cells under them, so it is left for the owner.
- 2026-11-16: refined: P8-T6 tenth follow-up (owner: "Yes: Add --destructive-foreground", "Markdown table headers: leave it"). `--destructive-foreground` is a theme token: in the color editor's Core group right after `--destructive`, mapped as `text-destructive-foreground`, declared in Dark, Light and Sepia as `var(--background)` (the same text the two uses had, so nothing changes in the built-in themes), and held to 4.5:1 on `--destructive` in `CONTRAST_PAIRS`. The name-input error bubble and the title bar close button use it, and the pair scan enforces destructive like the others. A user theme file that omits it takes it from its base, so existing theme files keep working. The markdown table header cells stay `--muted` with `--foreground` text, by the owner's decision. A hole the new token exposed is closed for all pairs: a test now requires every enforced pair to be a theme token and to have a contrast check of at least 4.5 in `CONTRAST_PAIRS` (removing one had survived).

(1) Every token row in the color editor has a hover hint, the themed shadcn tooltip through `HoverHint` (the project rule for hover text; it wraps at 20rem, which fits a sentence or two), on the name and the `--token` code. The sentences live in `src/lib/themeTokenUsage.ts`, one per token, written from the code that uses each token (a script listed every file using each token through a Tailwind utility or `var(--token)`, and a few were read in place); a test fails when a token has none, one names a token that does not exist, or one is empty, over 260 characters or does not end as a sentence. They describe where the app uses a token, not what the preview shows. The hover target is an inline-block, because a name that wraps onto two lines has a smaller hit area than its bounding box (found in the real window: the pointer at the box center missed the label). (2) The "Based on" toggle group in the color editor is now the text "Based on Dark." with a short note ("Fixed when the theme was made: a color the file leaves out comes from it"). `setThemeBase` had no other caller, so it and its tests are removed with it (ISC-479 and part of ISC-488 and ISC-491 are marked superseded, ISC-541 replaces them); `git revert` of this commit brings them back. The "Start from" group in the New theme form is unchanged. The top sentence of the editor no longer names the base. Not done, offered: showing the close-window button hover and the name error bubble in the preview, so the token is visible there too.

- 2026-11-17: refined: P8-T6 thirteenth follow-up (owner: "the scrollbars are shown in each end every tab, when the tabs are distributed over multiple panel - display the scrollbars only for the active tab, or when hovering over an inactive tab's content area"). Read as: with several panes open, the pane that is focused (`data-active="true"` on its section, one at a time) shows scrollbar thumbs; every other pane hides them until the pointer is over its body. One global rule in `src/styles/tokens.css`, after the thumb rules (ISC-248): `[data-pane-id][data-active='false'] [data-pane-body]:not(:hover, :has([data-theme-preview])) ::-webkit-scrollbar-thumb { background-color: transparent }`. It reaches every scroller in a pane body (editor, Settings, image viewer, any panel in a tab) and nothing else: the tab strip bar was already hidden, the tree and menus are not in a pane body. The scrollbar belongs to its scroller, so pointing at the bar counts as pointing at the body. Only the thumb is cleared, so the 10 px gutter stays and the text does not move. The Theme preview is exempt: it sits in an unfocused pane while its theme is edited, to show these very colors, and with the rule it would show no thumb to judge. The find and folder-search ticks along the scrollbar are not changed. Not done and a choice, not a limit: no fade, the thumb appears and goes at once (a fade on a WebKit scrollbar part was not tried); a single pane never changes. Revert: `git revert` the commit.

- 2026-11-17: refined: P8-T6 fifteenth follow-up (owner: "we have a setting to Limit the content width - make that setting available in the quick action menu (CTRL+Shift+P on Windows) and assign a keybinding to it + add it to the keybindings settings"). A new command `view.toggleContentWidth`, "Toggle Limit Content Width", in the View group, built exactly like Toggle Word Wrap: its state is `editor.limitContentWidth` (`isToggleOn` and `ToggleKey` in `features/settings/quick.ts` gained the key), and running it flips the setting through `flipSetting`, the Settings screen's own path, so unsaved edits in the settings.json tab are asked about first and the text column follows at once (`updateContentWidth` already runs on every settings change). The command palette is the quick action menu: it lists every registered command, with its key and On or Off. The default key is Alt+Shift+W: W for width, in the Alt+Shift family the app already uses (split right 2, split down 8, strikethrough 5), no other command has it (a test checks every default key is unique), and it does not use Ctrl+W or Ctrl+Shift+W, which close a tab and all tabs. VS Code's Alt+Z for word wrap was not copied: word wrap has no key here and this command is the owner's. "Add it to the keybindings settings" needed no code: the Keyboard category lists every registered command by group and its keys can be rebound there or in keybindings.json, so the command is there under View, found by "width" or by its key. Not added: a menu entry (the hamburger menu is a curated list and the owner named the palette and the key), a toolbar button, or a change to the setting itself. Revert: `git revert` the commit.
- 2026-11-17: refined: review of the twelfth to fifteenth follow-ups (advisor). (1) The command is now titled "Toggle Limit Content Width", not "Toggle Content Width Limit": the palette matches the words of a search in order, so the owner's own wording ("limit content width", the setting's label) found nothing under the old title, while "content width" did. The new title is the setting's label with Toggle in front, and a test searches it five ways. (2) With word wrap off the command changes the setting and nothing visible follows: the limit is the width of wrapped text (documentWidth.ts says so, and the setting's description says "Not used while long lines scroll sideways"), the Settings switch is not disabled in that case either, and a real run read the column at 2613 px with the limit on or off. Left as it is, to be told to the owner; making the command `enabled` only while wrap is on is the alternative. (3) The checks that the first runs skipped were done: see the verification entry.
- 2026-11-17: refined: P8-T6 sixteenth follow-up (owner: "toggling Limit Content Width when it's off and Word Wrap is off, it will not do anything visually... when Limit Content Width becomes enabled we also need to make sure that Word Wrap is also enabled"). The rule is in `changeSetting` in `features/settings/store.ts`, the one place every change comes through (the Settings switch, the command, a row's reset, the answer to the unsaved-edits question), so those four cannot disagree: when `editor.limitContentWidth` is set to on, `editor.wordWrap` is set to on in the same text edit and the same write. On is the default for word wrap, so its key leaves the file; comments and every other key stay. Turning the limit off, or changing any other setting, leaves word wrap alone, and a hand edit of settings.json is never rewritten (a file with wrap off and the limit on stays as typed; the limit then shows nothing, as before). Why not the command only: the owner said "when Limit Content Width becomes enabled", and the switch on the Settings screen has the same problem, so the rule sits under both. Not done, a choice: turning word wrap off while the limit is on does not turn the limit off (not asked, and the limit is a preference that should come back with wrap), and there is no notice when wrap is turned on too: the Word wrap switch and the palette state show it. The setting's description now says so ("It needs word wrap, so turning it on turns word wrap on too"), which is why a search for "wrap" in Settings now finds both rows. Revert: `git revert` the commit.

- 2026-11-20: refined: P9.1-T3 (owner request: zoom the whole window with Ctrl+plus, Ctrl+minus, Ctrl+0 and Ctrl+wheel). (1) The plan's "extra chords" do not exist: a command has one default shortcut, and `Mod++` is not text `parseKeybinding` reads. Zoom In has `Mod+=` as its shortcut, and `zoomAliasCommand` in `workbench/keyboard.ts` also takes a plus typed with Ctrl (Ctrl+Shift+= and the numpad plus) for it, so the alias follows the command and is not rebindable. The numpad minus and zero type the same characters as the main row, so they need none. A held zoom key keeps stepping; other shortcuts still run once. (2) The webview does the scaling (`getCurrentWebview().setZoom`, one host method `setZoom`, the capability `core:webview:allow-set-webview-zoom`), not CSS `zoom`: at 150 percent a real mouse click landed within one pixel of its aim and put the caret in the clicked character, and a real tab drag reordered the tabs. The pixel ratio includes the zoom (1.5 times 1.5 is 2.25), so the native drop code that divides by it stays right in principle. (3) The level is `view.zoom`, validated by `numberIn(50, 300, true)`: a hand-written 105 is allowed and a step snaps it to the grid. Ctrl+wheel and the keys step from the last level asked for, not from the setting, since a burst asks before the first write is done. The first call to the web view always sends, so a window the web view left zoomed starts at the setting. (4) The Zoom row sits in a new group Window on the Appearance screen, after Fonts. ISC-462 and ISC-464 name the headings of that screen, so both are reworded to add Window; `tests/features/commentedFiles.test.ts` shows why it is not first: it uncomments the first `// },` of the settings template, which has to be the Fonts group's. (5) The percentage shows in the status bar for 1.5 seconds after a change; that line has no other writer while no find bar is open.
- 2026-11-20: refined: P9.1-T5 reverses part of ISC-363 (owner request: inline code keeps the code font but takes the content font size, and the code size is for blocks). ISC-363 said `fonts.codeSize` sizes "code blocks and inline code"; it now says code blocks only, and its Test Strategy probe reads "code blocks are 18 px, inline code and body unchanged". The requirement changed, so the claim is not softened to pass; the old inline half is gone on purpose. Inline code in text and in a table cell is `font-size: 1em` (the code family stays), so it follows its surroundings: the body size in a paragraph, the heading's size in a heading, the cell's 0.95 in a table. The `--doc-code-ratio` property now has one reader, the code block rule `.cm-md-box-code`. Not done, a choice: the default `codeSize` stays 14 (blocks look as before; inline code in body text grows from 14 to 15 px); the Settings row says "The size of code blocks, in pixels. Inline code follows the text size." New claims ISC-563 (the rules) and ISC-564 (computed sizes). Revert: `git revert` the commit.
- 2026-11-20: refined: P9.1-T7 (owner request: pin and unpin tabs). (1) The pin is a prefix count `pinned` on the pane, not a flag per tab, so "pinned tabs come first" holds by construction and a move cannot break it; `moveTab` clamps the index into the dragged tab's own group. (2) The new Close rule changes ISC-307: Close All, Close Others and Close to the Right leave pinned tabs open, so Close All no longer always drops the pane; ISC-307 says so now, and the Close All command is off when only pinned tabs are left. Close on one tab, from the menu or Ctrl+W, still closes a pinned tab (it asks when the document has unsaved edits). (3) The count is saved inside the layout text and left out at zero, so the session version stays 1 and an older file reads as nothing pinned. (4) The Theme preview is never saved, so a pinned preview leaves the count one lower on restore. (5) A pinned tab shows the pin in the slot of the X; the dirty dot is smaller and sits on the pin's corner, and hides while the pointer is on the pin, which shows the unpin icon and unpins on a click.
- 2026-11-20: refined: P9.1-T8 (owner request: file-type icons in the sidebar). (1) ISC-105 said non-Markdown files "show dimmed"; the name is still dimmed (`text-muted-foreground`), but the icon is no longer faded: it is the coloured icon of its type, and a faded coloured icon reads as a mistake. (2) The icons are one SVG sprite in `public/` drawn with `<use>`, not one file per icon or inline markup: a single local request that the CSP (`img-src 'self'`) allows, no `data:` URI (which Vite makes of small assets and the CSP refuses) and no `v-html` (which `no-unsafe-html` forbids). (3) The icons come from the `material-icon-theme` npm package, which is not a dependency: `scripts/build-file-icons.mjs` packs the ids that `src/features/tree/fileIcons.ts` lists from an unpacked copy, so the sprite and the map cannot drift (a test compares them). (4) A new-file row being named keeps the Lucide file icon, since there is no name to read yet. (5) The Inter font's licence is still open under Remaining Work; this one ships as `public/file-icons-LICENSE.txt` and goes into the installer with it.

(2) The sidebar header labels were not level with the tab labels: the tab strip has `pt-2` and the header had none, so the label centers were 51 and 55.5 px, 4.5 px apart in all three themes. The header now has the same top padding, and 2 px more than that (`pt-2.5`): its labels are 12 px against the tabs' 13 px, and centered in the same space their baseline was still one pixel high (measured by the lowest row of ink: 59 against 60). - 2026-11-21: P9.1-T10 spike, built first and then run by the owner in a real window, where it worked. Decision: the `drag` crate 2.1.1 (MIT or Apache-2.0, windows only) rather than raw OLE. It starts an OLE drag of `CF_HDROP` files with the copy effect only and Escape to cancel, which are the plan's rules, and it is the crate the Tauri drag plugins use from a pointer handler, so a start from a pointer event with the button down is a known path. Raw OLE would be 300 lines of locally opted-in unsafe code. The price is two crates that were not in the build, `windows` 0.52 and `windows-core` 0.58 (the project's own `windows-core` is 0.62), plus `Cargo.lock`. The hand-over: the page's pointer drag, when the pointer goes past an edge of the window, ends itself and calls `drag_out`; Rust checks every path with `check_in_root` (so a file opened by hand is refused), and `drag_out` runs the drag on the main thread through `run_on_main_thread` because OLE's drag is modal and `OleInitialize` is per thread, then answers dropped or cancelled. Own-window drops: Rust marks a drag out under way in `DropRegistry` and `record` ignores drops meanwhile, and the page ignores file-drop events for 500 ms after, since the event can lag. The `Platform` trait has `drag_files_out` with an Unsupported default, so Linux and macOS compile and say so. Not decided by tests, only by the owner's window: whether WebView2's mouse capture lets OLE start from a page pointer drag, what the drag image looks like (none is given, so the shell's), and the cursor. If it does not start, the fallback is the same command called from a `dragstart` on a draggable element, which needs the tree rows to use HTML5 drag, which Windows blocks while native drops are on (see AGENTS.md), so then the task moves to Phase 10 with this row as the finding.
