- 2026-11-21: P9.1-T11 done as planned, with two decisions. (1) The indent is kept by a stand-in: an indented table's widget is a flex row of an invisible `span` that holds the typed whitespace (`white-space: pre`, so a tab follows the editor's tab size) and the scrolling table, so the gap is as wide as the text it replaces in any font. A top-level table keeps its old DOM, with no stand-in and an empty indent, so its tests and look do not move. (2) A table is drawn in a list only when its first row starts the line after whitespace; a marker on that line (`- | a |`) leaves it Markdown, and anything with a quote above it is skipped before the scan looks at it. The scan now descends only through `BulletList`, `OrderedList` and `ListItem`, and handles `Table` there and nothing else, so a picture or a paragraph in an item is as before. The rescan in the field needed no change: an edit already grows to the whole top-level list, which the random-edit test confirms. One thing found on the way: the random-edit test also fails on the old scan, at seed 7 round 31, where the incremental update missed a table that a fresh scan drew (a table after a list); it passes on the new code over 750 rounds and I did not trace the cause, so it may be an old miss the change happens to avoid.

Found by running the real block layer, not from the parser alone: the parser reads a table with 0 to 3 leading spaces as a table and with 4 spaces or a tab as an indented code block (CommonMark, so no change there), and `scanBlocks` draws top-level tables only, so a table inside a `ListItem` is never reached. - 2026-11-21: P9.1-T27 done, with these decisions (also in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09.1/decisions.md`). (1) Git is run as a program (no new crate): `git --no-optional-locks -c core.fsmonitor=false -C <repo> status --porcelain=v2 -z --ignored=matching`, with no input, no error output, no console window, a 20 s timeout and at most four runs alive in the whole app. (2) `core.fsmonitor` is forced off because a repository's own config could name a program there and `status` would run it, which breaks "Markdown content is untrusted" for a folder opened from outside; a test sets one and proves it is not run. The clean and process filters a repository can name cannot be switched off by one option, so that risk stays and is written in the architecture doc (`git.enabled` off closes it). (3) A `git.path` that names no file falls through to the PATH and the standard folders instead of stopping the search. (4) A deletion or type change counts as modified, an intent-to-add file and a copy as added. (5) The command answers `{repos, entries}` with each entry tagged by its repository's index, drops what an outer repository says at or inside an inner one, and drops what a repository above the root says outside it. (6) `git.path` has one spelling on both sides, pinned by `tests/data/git-paths.json`, which both the page and Rust tests read. (7) No page-side host call for `git_status` yet: T28 adds it with the colours.
- 2026-11-21: P9.1-T28 done, with these decisions (also in `ai-docs/phases/phase-09.1/decisions.md`). (1) The page asks `git_status(root, folders)` once per open root (host call `gitStatus`, read by `parseGitReport` so a malformed report fails there and shows nothing). The asking and the colours live in two new files beside the tree store: `gitModel.ts` (pure: a report becomes an index, and drawing a row is lookups) and `gitStore.ts` (the triggers, the 250 ms debounce, the newest-question rule). (2) Rules: untracked and added are green, modified and renamed blue, a conflict the destructive colour, ignored muted; everything inside an untracked or ignored folder takes the folder's status (also a folder entry above the root); every status but ignored makes the folders above it blue unless they have a status of their own; a root row takes only its own or an inherited status and is never blue for what is below; an entry counts for a path only when its repository is the path's innermost one. (3) Triggers: a root opening, restoring or closing (a watch on the roots), the window focus, a save or save as, a create, rename, move, delete and copy-in through the tree, a tree-watcher event, and `git.enabled` or `git.path` changing. Opening a folder is not a trigger, so a repository deeper than three levels shows at the next one. (4) Three hooks exist because the host allows one tree listener and `documents/store` cannot import the tree: `onTreeChanged` and `onTreeReset` in the tree store, `onDocumentSaved` in the documents store. (5) Tokens `git-added`, `git-modified`, `git-ignored` in the sidebar group in all five places; two `destructive` pairs on the sidebar and its selected row go beyond the plan. (6) With git off the page makes no call and clears the colours after the debounce; a failed call or a report that does not parse is the same as git saying nothing.

Since 2026-11-11 the control is an injected 40 px jump (read back as 40 px over 20 steps); the real widget still moves 0.47 px up and 0.25 px down.
- ISC-72 (coverage note 2026-11-08, by me): the `scroll-stability` scenario walks its own image document, which has no table over 50 rows, so its pass does not cover the two-step table. That case was measured separately in the debug app and is in decisions.md, "ISC-6 fixed": with an idle-time swap the line under a big table moved up to 107 px; with the swap in the write phase it did not in 14 runs. This is debug-app evidence, not the release scenario.
- ISC-182: `tests/editor/images.test.ts` loads, fails and refuses an image and reads the same fixed height (160 px block, 20 px inline) before and after, and a remembered size reserves the real aspect ratio from the first frame; the real window showed the 160 px dashed placeholder with its alt text for a missing file (P4-T2).
- ISC-68: `pnpm test:web tests/editor/frontmatter.test.ts` (34 tests): the block at the very start of the text, `---` to `---` or `...` within 200 lines, is one pill labelled "Frontmatter" (it listed its top-level keys until ISC-238); a caret touching it, edges included, brings the YAML back as plain muted text and the pill returns when the caret leaves; typing the closing line far below turns it on; the block layer and the inline layer draw and style nothing inside it. The real window (debug app, trusted mouse) showed the pill `Frontmatter title, tags, draft` with the caret away and the plain YAML with the caret at the top; the first look showed the YAML styled as a heading by the highlighter, which is why open blocks now get a plain line class (P4-T3).
- ISC-69: `pnpm test:web tests/editor/checkbox.test.ts` presses a box and compares the file a save would write: one byte differs, the space or the `x`, for LF, CRLF, CR and a file mixing all three, and undo restores the file byte for byte, two quick presses on one box undoing one at a time. The real window (trusted click, Ctrl+S, bytes read from disk): one byte for an LF file (checked to open), a CRLF file and a file with 9 CRLF, 7 LF and 1 CR; Ctrl+Z then Ctrl+S gave the original bytes every time (P4-T3).
- ISC-184: the same file, a document made read-only through `Document`: two presses change no text, leave nothing to undo and the document unedited; the real window opened a file with invalid UTF-8 as read-only (`aria-readonly`), two trusted clicks on its boxes changed nothing and the file on disk was identical after Ctrl+S. 26 deliberate breaks across the T3 code were all caught; the first 21 left 6 alive, which led to a stricter scan edge, two redundant pieces removed and four tests tightened or added (P4-T3).
- ISC-70: `pnpm test:web tests/editor/codeHighlight.test.ts` (20 tests) opens fenced blocks in js, ts, python, py, json, css, sh, go and rust and reads the class each token gets from the style: keywords, strings, numbers and comments get four different classes, the block is plain until its parser has loaded, a fence word written in capitals works, the text of the block is the text of the file, and typing inside a block is colored again. The colors are `var(--syntax-*)` names only, and each exists in both theme files. Real window (debug app on the dev server, 2026-11-08): ts, python, rust, css, json and sh blocks colored by token, an unknown word and a fence with no word left as plain text, document length 702 equal to the file size; typing `7` after `42` in a block gave `427` still colored as a number and Ctrl+Z restored 702; raw mode (Ctrl+E) shows the same colors. Interceptor on `pnpm dev` through the mock host: the screenshot shows the same colors in the dark theme; in the light theme the computed colors are `oklch(0.48 0.15 310)` for keywords, `oklch(0.46 0.11 145)` for strings and `oklch(0.5 0.13 50)` for numbers, but the DOM-render screenshot washed them out so the light theme was checked by value, not by eye (P4-T4).
- ISC-71: `node scripts/check-arch.mjs languages-lazy --dist <build>` on a build made with `vite build --sourcemap`: the chunks the page loads first (the entry script, the modulepreload link and what they import statically, 2 files, 790 kB) hold `lang-css`, `lang-html`, `lang-javascript` and their grammars and no other language package, while the language chunks and the list of them load on demand. The claim as written, "no parser in the entry bundle", is not literally true and cannot be: `@codemirror/lang-markdown` imports `lang-html` (which brings `lang-css` and `lang-javascript`) for the HTML it finds in Markdown. The rule allows those six packages by name and nothing else; the allowance is a constant with a comment that says why. Source half: no static import of a parser package in `src/`. Runtime half: `tests/editor/codeLanguagesLazy.test.ts` counts imports of `@codemirror/language-data` and sees none for documents with no fence word, `~~~` with a blank word, or an unlabeled fence, and exactly one for two views holding three languages. In the real window the language list was not requested for a document with no fenced block and was requested after a document with one was opened. 9 deliberate breaks of the rule (ignore modulepreload, skip static imports, allow the list, drop `lang-css` from the allowance, widen the allowance, Windows paths, pnpm store paths, `explicit` flag, skip scanning) were all caught by `tests/scripts/checkArch.test.ts` (92 tests); 4 breaks of the editor code (no extension lookup, no `codeLanguages`, an unknown token name in the style, a static import of the list) were caught by the two test files (P4-T4).
- ISC-58 (closed again 2026-11-08, P4-T4 perf re-measure, owner approved the scenario change): on the T4 build the unmodified scenario stopped with "the line below the span is not in the view" because it read the view right after `forceParsing`, while the block widgets the parse draws had moved the line 199,000 px away until CodeMirror re-anchored. With `settleOnLine` (commit e564f1c) `pnpm perf reveal` on the release app: work per leave p95 4.10, 4.00 and 4.00 ms against 16 ms, five runs each, repeatable within 5 to 8 percent, and 4.00 ms (3.9 to 4.3) in the full named set on e564f1c. Phase 3 measured 3.7 to 4.0 ms. See decisions.md, "Perf diagnosis after your answers".
- ISC-63 (closed again 2026-11-08, P4-T4 perf re-measure, owner approved the scenario change): since T1 the same-work check had failed in every run, because the scenario read the view right after `forceParsing` and the two views showed different text (visible characters 4541 against 6605 at the late spot at T4). With `settleOnLine` and one fixed 5000-character scan range in both documents (commit e564f1c), `pnpm perf decorations-scale` on the release app: worst deviation 0.198, 0.192, 0.212 and 0.2155 against the 0.35 limit (an earlier settle variant 0.182, 0.210, 0.207), same work in both places every time, control 8 to 9 times. Phase 3 had 0.15 to 0.28. The limit was not changed.
- ISC-6 (reopened 2026-11-08, by me): closed earlier at 11.2 ms (see the entry above), and not true at HEAD. `pnpm perf tab-switch` on the release app, five runs each: switch p95 52.7 ms (51.6 to 53.3) on e564f1c, 49.6 ms (48.4 to 51.0) on d5166ab (T4), 52.4 ms (52.1 to 55.1) on 708a90c (T1), 16.1 ms on c98e4d0 (end of Phase 3); against 50 ms. The first build over it is T1, the block layer (T2 and T3 not measured: the bisect was stopped by low memory). The limit was not changed. See decisions.md, "Final perf set on e564f1c". Cause measured 2026-11-08: switching to the tables-heavy fixture draws a 2,907-cell table whole (51 to 56 ms median against 15 ms with the layer off); drawing only its first 40 rows brings it to 20 ms. Fix proposed in decisions.md, "ISC-6 tab-switch: profile in the real window", awaiting the owner.
- ISC-6 (closed again 2026-11-08, owner approved the fix, commits 4a1383d, 2fe9bdf and 9d6f8e9): a table with more than 50 body rows is built as its header and first 50 rows plus one spacer row at the height of the rest; after idle time the real rows are swapped in during CodeMirror's write phase, with a measure request keyed by the table's own wrapper (`src/editor/livePreview/blocks/widgets.ts`). `pnpm perf tab-switch` on a fresh release build of 9d6f8e9, five runs: switch p95 18.7 ms (runs 20.3, 18.7, 18.7, 18.2, 20.2; repeatable) against 50 ms; median 15.6 ms, plain 16.3, with a change made elsewhere 14.5, synchronous part 3.6 ms. Before the fix: 52.7 ms. An earlier run of the same code before the per-table key (2fe9bdf) read 19.7 ms and the harness called it not repeatable (spread 11%); it is superseded. The cause was measured in the real window: the first screen of `tables-heavy-1mb.md` is one table of 2,907 cells that the view laid out whole (51 to 56 ms median for that switch against 15 ms with the block layer off; 17 to 18.5 ms after the change). Tests: `tests/editor/tableProgressive.test.ts` (10 tests); I broke the widget ten ways on purpose (every row at once, spacer never replaced, no re-measure, no cancel on destroy, click ignoring the row, one row short, spacer too short, rest in reverse order, swap outside the write step, one measure key shared by all tables) and each failed a test. A real mouse click on a row not yet drawn landed on the right source line. Limit unchanged. See decisions.md, "ISC-6 fixed".
- ISC-71 (reworded 2026-11-08 by the owner's decision, closed again): the claim was "Language parsers stay out of the entry bundle", which is not literally true because `@codemirror/lang-markdown` imports `lang-html`, `lang-css` and `lang-javascript` (and their Lezer parsers) itself. The owner chose to reword it to what the check enforces: "The entry bundle holds no language parser beyond the ones Markdown itself imports (HTML, CSS and JavaScript); every other language loads when a fence names it." Evidence is the entry above: the check reads the source map of every chunk the page loads first and fails on any other language package; its tests plant a parser in the entry and watch it fail.
- ISC-185 (closed 2026-11-08, owner request with two Obsidian screenshots, top-level fences only by the owner's answer; commit c85e3c7; the owner then used it in the app and said it works fine): `src/editor/livePreview/box.ts` and a `FencedCode` branch in `scan.ts`. Each line of the block gets container classes and draws its slice of the border and background with `::before`. Seen in the debug app (an editor mounted over the app page, so the app's own CSS and theme tokens apply), screenshots in both themes: bordered, rounded, subtle border, gap above and below. Not seen through File > Open in a release build. Tests: `tests/editor/codeBlock.test.ts` (21 tests). See decisions.md, "Code blocks drawn as a container".
- ISC-186 (closed 2026-11-08): with the caret outside, the opening fence line and the closing fence are hidden and the first line carries `data-box-label` (the list's name, `TypeScript` for `ts`; the word as typed when the list does not know it; none without a word). - ISC-187 (closed 2026-11-08): a caret at the first character, inside, or at the end of the block shows both fences and drops the label; a selection that only touches the block does the same. Unit tests, and the real window (fences muted, no label, same box).
- ISC-188 (closed 2026-11-08): in the debug app the height of every line of the fixture, and the whole content height (771.9 px), were identical with the caret outside and inside the first block; no difference on any line. The fence lines are lines in both states.
- ISC-189 (closed 2026-11-08): `boxLines` takes a `kind`; tests call it with `mermaid` and get the same container classes, the label on the first line only, and lines for the visible range only (a 5,000-line block gives 51 lines for a 51-line view). I broke the code seven ways on purpose (last line as mid, fences hidden while shown, no visible-range limit, closing fence taken as the last mark, nested fences allowed, label on every line, one shared line decoration for different labels) and each failed a test.

The bar says "Count stopped" when time ran out with nothing found, never "No results". Real window (`count-timing.mjs`, the debug build's WebView2, 10,850,028 characters, five runs each): a rare word 20.4 to 23.3 ms, a word that is absent 20.7 to 25.7 ms, the letter "e" 9.8 to 13.1 ms (stopped at the 10,000 cap). A first version with a 40 ms budget and 512 KB pieces measured 51 to 71 ms for the same words, which is why the budget is 20 ms and the pieces 128 KB. The count runs once per change of query or selection and after a 250 ms pause in typing, so on a file this large a rare word costs about 25 ms of main thread per Enter in the box; typing during an open bar is measured by `typing-find-open` at the end of Phase 7.

  Breaks that failed a test, each put back afterwards: 25 changes on the page (a hex of five digits accepted, hue above 360 accepted, an edit of an unknown token, applying with no second check, the dark class from the palette name, a missing theme reported before the folder was read, a built-in-named file not ignored, an own write read as a new one, Customize writing with `create` off, no debounce, the write skipped, the copy not selected, a picker for a color with alpha, no contrast warnings, a typed value not checked, the Theme row without user themes, the notice hidden, the settings store not applying the theme, `loadThemes` after `showWindow`, a name allowing `..`, Sepia's background changed, Dark's selector lost, `data-theme` gone from `index.html`, the mock replacing on create, System ignoring the OS) and 10 in Rust (`..` allowed, create replacing, write making files, no size cap on read, no size cap on write, no file cap, folders listed, no `.json` filter, names unchecked in the list, a watcher that hears every name); all caught. Two survivors led to better tests: the own-write echo (the test now checks a newer picker color is not rolled back, which also found a real flaw: the write's landing re-applied the older file color, fixed with `showPendingColors`) and the name order (NTFS lists in order already, so the sort is now a pure function with its own test; three breaks of it are caught).
  Real window (debug exe with the new Rust, my own Vite on 1420, own data folder and WebView2 profile, real key, mouse and text events over the debugging port): Dark, Light and Sepia read by eye on the Settings Appearance page and on an editor tab holding headings, emphasis, a quote, lists, a link, inline code and a TypeScript block (and at 3 times size for the code block); the other four Settings groups in Dark, Light and Sepia. Writing `settings.json` while the app ran switched the theme each time with no restart. Customize on Dark made `Dark copy.json` with 57 tokens, selected it, and the editor showed 57 rows in Core, Sidebar, Chrome, Editor and Syntax; typing `#ff8800` into Primary wrote it to the file and applied it; `red` showed the reason and left the file; a foreground of `#2a2f38` showed "Foreground on Background: contrast 1.21, it should be at least 7." and was written anyway, and fixing it cleared the line; a hand edit of the file's background reached the screen; a broken file showed Dark and a notice naming line 6, column 15, and the fixed file brought the theme back; deleting the selected file showed Dark and left `settings.json` as it was, and writing it back restored the theme; `sepia.json` in the folder was ignored with its notice. System followed the emulated OS appearance dark, light, dark (`Emulation.setEmulatedMedia`) and Customize from System copied Dark, then Light (`System copy`, `System copy 2`, the base and the background matching). Open themes folder created the folder, opened Explorer on it, and I closed that one window.
  Not checked: a real change of the Windows theme (the OS appearance was emulated through the DevTools port); the native frame and WebView2 background before the first paint (the order is pinned by a test and the window opens hidden, but no frame was captured); the color picker's own dialog (only its input event, in jsdom); the symbolic-link case of the Rust store (the test returns early when the account cannot make a link, and it could not here), so a file that is a link is handled by `file_type` and `symlink_metadata` by reading, not by a run; a theme file with Windows line endings in the window (a unit test covers the edit); a window narrower than 1100 pixels on the editor; the toasts: warnings persist and stack over the right side of the Theme colors header, where Customize sits, so a user with a few bad theme files could find the button covered until the toasts are closed (it stopped a driver click in the real run; not fixed in this task).

- P9.1-T2 (2026-11-20): ISC-555 and ISC-556 closed. The owner found that Ctrl+R reloaded the whole app. The web view's own accelerator keys are now off for the main window: `Platform::disable_browser_keys` (default no-op) is called from `setup` in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`, and the Windows implementation in `src-tauri/src/platform/windows/mod.rs` sets `ICoreWebView2Settings3::SetAreBrowserAcceleratorKeysEnabled(false)` through `WebviewWindow::with_webview`. Tauri 2.12 does not expose that setting on its window builder, though wry does, hence the two Windows-only dependencies `webview2-com` 0.39 and `windows-core` 0.62 (the versions Tauri already builds). Real window, own rig: `pnpm tauri dev` with `--remote-debugging-port=9222`, a marker and a key logger set on the page over CDP, then keys sent with SendKeys. Result: Ctrl+R, F5, Ctrl+Shift+R, Ctrl+U, F7, Ctrl+plus (twice), Ctrl+minus and Ctrl+0 all reached the page as key events, the marker stayed, `performance.timeOrigin` did not change and `devicePixelRatio` stayed 1.5 (Windows display scale), so nothing reloaded or zoomed; Ctrl+P then opened the quick-open palette. Not checked: the same keys with the setting off (no control run on the old build; the owner reported the Ctrl+R reload), F12 with the `devtools` setting on (the page asks for it through `devtools_open`, which calls `open_devtools` and is not an accelerator), and Mod+F and copy and paste. `pnpm check:rust` passes.

## Remaining Work

- [x] A block layer edge case found while fixing ISC-334: a table that a selection touches is not drawn, so nothing remembers how far it reached. An edit that makes it swallow the lines after it (a picture two lines below becomes a row) leaves that picture drawn, and an edit that makes it let them go leaves a picture undrawn until the next change near it. Reached only by a random-edit property test (seed 11, picture-and-table document); not seen in use. Fixing it needs the field to keep each hidden block's extent. Not part of Phase 9. Fixed in Phase 9 (P9-T5 follow-up): see the Decisions row of 2026-11-13.
